```
                                   `pingDatabase()` is one `SELECT 1` and that
                                   is the whole query surface, because Story 2.2
                                   owns the rest. Created by index.ts and NOT by
                                   buildServer(), because nothing serves data
                                   yet and every test that builds a server would
                                   otherwise have to fake one; the reversal
                                   trigger is Story 2.8's first route that needs
                                   it. `new Pool()` is LAZY, which is what lets
                                   its tests be fast ones. The `pool.on("error")`
                                   handler is NOT optional: an EventEmitter with
                                   no error listener throws, so without it a
                                   dropped idle connection is an
                                   uncaughtException, an exit 1, and a
                                   crash-loop on a liveness-probed platform.
                                   `connectionTimeoutMillis` is NOT optional
                                   either: pg's default is 0, meaning wait
                                   forever. Also where the DATABASE_SSL vocabulary
                                   is mapped onto pg's options, and where the
                                   `entra` credential branch is FILLED since
                                   Task 2.1.6 and calls entra-token.ts. The
                                   credential stays a FUNCTION rather than a
                                   value because `pg` calls it once per
                                   CONNECTION — so the token is minted per
                                   connection, which is the shape a credential
                                   valid for 24 hours wants, and it is why
                                   there is deliberately no cache here.
                                   Since Task 2.1.7 it also holds the BOUND the
                                   diagnostic endpoint reads through —
                                   `createCachedDatabaseCheck`, a TTL plus
                                   SINGLE FLIGHT, so the query rate this
                                   application can be driven to produce is one
                                   per DIAGNOSTIC_CACHE_TTL_MS and one in-flight
                                   query whatever the caller count. Single
                                   flight is the half that matters: 25
                                   concurrent requests on a cold pool would
                                   otherwise be 25 connections and 25 token
                                   mints. DIAGNOSTIC_CACHE_TTL_MS (5 s) is
                                   deliberately BELOW POOL_IDLE_TIMEOUT_MS
                                   (10 s, pg's own default restated explicitly)
                                   — a SHORTER bound is CHEAPER, because cost
                                   steps at the idle timeout rather than scaling
                                   with frequency. A test asserts that ordering
    src/entra-token.ts             minting the deployed database credential
                                   (Task 2.1.6): one Microsoft Entra access
                                   token, from the container app's own managed
                                   identity, used verbatim as the PostgreSQL
                                   password. The SECOND file in the workspace
                                   that reads process.env, and that is the
                                   design rather than a lapse — IDENTITY_HEADER
                                   is itself a bearer credential, so putting it
                                   on Config would break the structural
                                   no-credential guarantee config.ts records.
                                   Neither identity variable is in
                                   CONFIG_VARIABLES or .env.example, on purpose.
                                   IDENTITY_ENDPOINT with X-IDENTITY-HEADER and
                                   NEVER 169.254.169.254, which is the virtual
                                   machine recipe and does not exist inside a
                                   container app — measured, the real value is
                                   a localhost sidecar. TOKEN_TIMEOUT_MS is 3 s
                                   and has to stay strictly BELOW database.ts's
                                   CONNECT_TIMEOUT_MS, or a hung metadata
                                   endpoint is reported as a generic connection
                                   timeout and never names itself; a test
                                   asserts that ordering. No message it can
                                   produce contains the header, the token or a
                                   response body — a 200 body IS the credential
    src/routes/health.ts           GET /health, its response schema and the
                                   `satisfies` guard. It no longer declares the
                                   response TYPE: Task 1.12.1 moved
                                   HealthResponse and HealthStatus to
                                   packages/shared and imports them back, which
                                   is the FIRST import of that package from this
                                   one — the manifest has declared it since
                                   Story 1.1 without importing it. The schema
                                   stays here, because JsonSchemaProperty is
                                   deliberately not shared, and it now declares
                                   `enum: HEALTH_STATUSES` rather than a second
                                   literal ["ok"]
    src/routes/diagnostics.ts      GET /diagnostics/database (Task 2.1.7), and
                                   the answer to Story 2.1's fourth open
                                   decision: `/health` says NOTHING about the
                                   database and this says it instead. The
                                   SECOND file in routes/ — the directory Task
                                   1.2.3 created for a second route that took
                                   eleven stories to arrive.
                                   NO probe uses it, no timer drives it and the
                                   frontend never calls it. All three platform
                                   probes point at `/health`, and a failing
                                   LIVENESS probe kills the replica — so a
                                   `/health` that fails on an unreachable
                                   database turns a recoverable outage into a
                                   restart loop. A READINESS surface is refused
                                   too, and for a different reason: `Single`
                                   revision mode at `minReplicas: 1` means an
                                   unready replica is not a degraded service, it
                                   is NO service.
                                   It always answers 200, including when the
                                   database is down — the question was answered
                                   correctly, and a 503 would need the
                                   `SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE` code database.ts
                                   reserves for Story 2.8. The body carries
                                   whether, how long and how stale, and NO error
                                   message, host, port or SQLSTATE, because the
                                   ingress is public and unauthenticated; the
                                   reason goes to the log at `warn` and
                                   `x-request-id` joins the two. What actually
                                   holds that shut is the SCHEMA rather than the
                                   handler — measured, see the test.
                                   Registered from index.ts and NOT by
                                   buildServer(), because the pool takes
                                   `app.log` so the app precedes the pool and
                                   the pool precedes this route. That
                                   deliberately does NOT fire database.ts's
                                   recorded reversal trigger; the cost is that
                                   server.test.ts's route-table walk cannot see
                                   it, so its `500: apiErrorSchema` is asserted
                                   in its own test
    vitest.process.config.ts       the SECOND runner in this package (Task
                                   1.10.5), and the only package with two. Its
                                   `include` and the unit config's `exclude`
                                   are one decision: they partition
                                   `src/**/*.test.ts` between them. No coverage
                                   block, and that is a finding rather than an
                                   omission — see below
    src/index.process.test.ts      the process half: signals, exit codes, the
                                   ceiling, the second signal, EADDRINUSE and
                                   both crash handlers, against a spawned
                                   `dist/index.js`. The only test file in the
                                   workspace that needs a build to have run,
                                   and the only one that never asserts a timing
    .env.example                   the documented variable set (Tasks 1.6.6
                                   and 1.7.1; five since Task 1.8.3's CORS_ORIGIN).
                                   Tracked, by the `!.env.example` negation;
                                   `.env` beside it is not. `pnpm env:check`
                                   fails if this and CONFIG_VARIABLES disagree,
                                   defaults included
  frontend/                        @marketpulse/frontend — React + Vite (Story 1.3)
    .storybook/                    the component workshop's configuration
      main.ts                      framework, story glob, addons, telemetry off.
                                   Reuses vite.config.ts rather than forking it,
                                   with ONE override since Task 1.11.6:
                                   `publicDir: false`, so the workshop stops
                                   copying apps/frontend/public/ — the deploy
                                   client globs for staticwebapp.config.json and
                                   was finding the workshop's copy. That is the
                                   `viteFinal` this file says it deliberately
                                   does not have, so it is a divergence with its
                                   reason beside it. `staticDirs: []` was tried
                                   and does NOT work
      preview.tsx                  the same three stylesheets main.tsx imports,
                                   in the same order, plus the same getTokens()
                                   startup assertion — and since Task 1.5.3 the
                                   MemoryRouter decorator, which is why it is
                                   .tsx. Both files are outside every tsconfig
                                   — see eslint.config.mjs
    .env.example                   VITE_API_BASE_URL since Task 1.11.5 — one
                                   story earlier than Story 1.6 predicted,
                                   because Story 1.11's criterion needs an
                                   address. Optional, defaulting to
                                   http://localhost:3000, which is a MATCHED
                                   PAIR with the backend's CORS_ORIGIN default
                                   of http://localhost:5173. It also still
                                   exists for the reason it always did: this is
                                   the file open in front of whoever is about to
                                   put an Alpaca key in apps/frontend/.env
    public/
      staticwebapp.config.json     the DEPLOYED FRONTEND'S host configuration
                                   (Task 1.11.4) — navigationFallback with
                                   exclude: ["/assets/*"], globalHeaders for the
                                   document's no-cache, and one route rule for
                                   the hashed assets' immutable year. In public/
                                   because Azure Static Web Apps requires it at
                                   the ROOT of the deployed output, and Vite
                                   copies public/ there untouched — so it is the
                                   fourth file in the artefact rather than a
                                   platform setting. Prettier reads it and
                                   nothing validates its schema. Storybook USED
                                   to copy public/ too, putting a second copy in
                                   storybook-static/ that the deploy client
                                   preferred; closed in Task 1.11.6 with
                                   `publicDir: false` in .storybook/main.ts
    index.html                     Vite's real entry point. At the package root,
                                   inside no tsconfig — Vite crawls its script
                                   tag to find the module graph
    vite.config.ts                 dev server, preview server, build target,
                                   React plugin. Also outside every tsconfig,
                                   hence its entry in eslint.config.mjs
    vitest.config.ts               the runner, and since Task 1.9.5 the
                                   coverage settings too. The ONLY one of the
                                   three that merges its package's build config,
                                   so the resolver that builds the app runs its
                                   tests — which is what makes CSS Modules real
                                   here. Both its `include` globs admit `.tsx`,
                                   unlike the other two, and that is load-bearing
    coverage/                      `pnpm coverage`'s output. Gitignored, and in
                                   every package. Never committed
    src/test-setup.ts              `afterEach(cleanup)`, and nothing else. Not
                                   application context — it exists because
                                   `globals: false` stops @testing-library
                                   registering its own
    src/test-render.tsx            the ONE description of the application's
                                   context for tests: MemoryRouter and an
                                   initial entry. Every provider Epic 2 adds
                                   lands here. Not used by App.test.tsx, which
                                   drives the real BrowserRouter
    src/**/*.test.tsx              tests beside their subject, components
                                   included — which is why check-stories.mjs
                                   now skips `.test.tsx`
    src/vite-env.d.ts              the TYPED half of the environment boundary
                                   (Task 1.11.5). Declares Vite's
                                   strictImportMetaEnv, which removes
                                   ImportMetaEnv's `any` index signature, so a
                                   misspelled VITE_ name is TS2551 rather than
                                   `undefined` at run time. Every variable the
                                   frontend reads must be declared here AND in
                                   .env.example; nothing checks that pair
    src/api-base-url.ts            where the API lives. resolveApiBaseUrl(raw)
                                   takes its input as a parameter for the reason
                                   loadConfig(env) does — import.meta.env is
                                   substituted at build time and cannot be
                                   varied by a test. Deliberately NOT folded
                                   into api-client.ts (Task 1.12.2): folding it
                                   in would put a module-load-time
                                   import.meta.env read inside the module every
                                   test of the client imports
    src/api-client.ts              the transport, and the ONLY file in this
                                   application that calls fetch (Task 1.12.2).
                                   Owns the base URL, the deadline, the abort
                                   signal, the ApiError parse and the
                                   correlation id. Still no React. API_TIMEOUT_MS
                                   is 5 s and is LOAD-BEARING on Task 1.12.1's
                                   vocabulary — a deadline that expires is
                                   `unreachable`, not `degraded`, because
                                   nothing arrived — and it has to stay strictly
                                   below Task 1.12.3's poll interval, which
                                   ~~nothing checks~~ a test in
                                   use-backend-health.test.ts now asserts. Seven outcomes, never a
                                   throw. Deliberately no retry: retry is a
                                   property of the poll. Also where the decision
                                   about how much of a requestId a user may ever
                                   see is written down
    src/use-backend-health.ts      the poll: the application's FIRST state,
                                   first effect and first network loop (Task
                                   1.12.3). Replaced health-probe.ts, which is
                                   deleted — it said it would be. Renders
                                   nothing. HEALTH_POLL_INTERVAL_MS is 30 s, a
                                   LITERAL and not a VITE_ variable, and it has
                                   to stay above api-client.ts's
                                   API_TIMEOUT_MS — an ordering nothing in
                                   `verify` checks, so a TEST asserts it.
                                   Schedules the next poll when the previous one
                                   SETTLES, so two requests can never overlap. A
                                   hidden tab does not poll and a returning one
                                   polls immediately, which is what an automated
                                   tab (reporting `hidden`) has to work around.
                                   No try/catch anywhere in it, deliberately:
                                   getHealth() never throws
    src/main.tsx                   the mount: createRoot + StrictMode. Task
                                   1.11.5 put one `void probeBackendHealth()`
                                   above it; Task 1.12.3 took it away with the
                                   module, so there is NO network call in this
                                   file — the poll starts from App
    src/App.tsx                    the router's host since Task 1.5.2:
                                   BrowserRouter, AppHeader, the five Routes.
                                   Not a page, and no navigation since 1.5.3.
                                   Since Task 1.12.3 it also CALLS
                                   useBackendHealth(), and since 1.12.5 it
                                   passes the result to AppHeader as FOUR props
                                   rather than one object — collapsing them is
                                   the thing to resist, not the tidy-up to make.
                                   The call is here rather than in AppHeader
                                   because the header sits inside its own
                                   ErrorBoundary: a header that throws would
                                   otherwise take the health check down at the
                                   moment it became most interesting. Every poll
                                   re-renders this whole tree and that is
                                   ACCEPTED rather than confined, with the
                                   argument beside the call; the reversal
                                   trigger is a second consumer or Epic 3's
                                   rate.
                                   Its `basename` reads import.meta.env.BASE_URL
                                   (Task 1.6.5) — the ONLY place the deployment
                                   path reaches the application
    src/App.module.css             the shell's styles — two rules, the ground
                                   and the gap. It has shrunk three times
    src/routes/                    one file per route, plus the shape they
                                   share. Deliberately NOT under components/:
                                   the stories check walks that directory and
                                   a placeholder owes no permutation grid
      paths.ts                     every path, once, as const. The mitigation
                                   for React Router's unchecked `to`
      MarketOverview.tsx           the landing route — and the home of Story
                                   1.4's render check since App stopped being
                                   a page. Deleting it drops ~100 kB. Since
                                   Task 1.5.4 it also holds §9's region grid,
                                   and the render check sits inside the
                                   primary region rather than beside it
      Placeholder.tsx              label, name, one sentence naming the epic
      NotFound.tsx                 a route, not a fallback. Not an error
    src/styles/tokens.css          the design tokens and the theming

    src/styles/market.css          the chromatic palette and the semantic
                                   market tokens over it (Task 1.4.4).

                                   the market: `--status-error` since 1.4.4 and
                                   `--service-*` since Task 1.12.4. The service
                                   trio resolves to the same two values the

                                   the point — the two indicators report facts
                                   that fail independently, so one changing
                                   colour must not move the other
    src/styles/base.css            color-scheme, the body ground, the default

    src/styles/tokens.ts           typed read-once access to the tokens, for

    src/cx.ts                      class-name composition; three lines that
                                   exist because of noUncheckedIndexedAccess
    src/report-error.ts            the ONLY place a render failure is reported

                                   here; there is nowhere to send it yet, and
                                   the argument against a window listener is
                                   written out beside the argument for the
                                   backend's crash handlers it would imitate
    src/components/                one directory per component, each holding
                                   <Name>.tsx, <Name>.module.css and
                                   <Name>.stories.tsx. One component per file —
                                   the stories check cannot see a second one
      Popover/                     the ONLY file importing @base-ui/react.
                                   A popover and not a tooltip; see below
      PriceChange/                 signed figure, arrow glyph, hidden word
      AnomalyBadge/                the band chip, its name inside the fill
      FeedIndicator/               marker shape plus word; stale is not an error
      SecurityRow/                 the representative component (Task 1.4.5),
                                   composing the four above into one <tr>
      AppHeader/                   the chrome (Task 1.5.3). The first component
                                   here that needs a router to render, and the
                                   first that is itself a landmark. Its status
                                   strip is THREE regions since Task 1.12.5 —
                                   market feed, backend service, market clock —
                                   with the service before the clock, because
                                   `.clock` is `align-items: flex-end` as the
                                   end of the strip and a region after it takes
                                   that edge away. Its AllPermutations story
                                   stopped being a cartesian product in the same
                                   change: five CHOSEN rows with the reason for
                                   each, which is also what keeps the two
                                   landmark rules disabled on one story rather
                                   than over twenty headers
      BackendIndicator/            the backend service's state (Task 1.12.4),
                                   and the answer to the one-indicator-or-two
                                   question Story 1.4 posed: a SECOND indicator
                                   beside FeedIndicator, not a widening of it.
                                   `FeedStatus` is what the backend REPORTS
                                   about the market feed; `BackendStatus` is
                                   what this client CONCLUDES about whether the
                                   backend answered at all. They fail
                                   independently and a user needs both answers.
                                   What is shared is the marker language — a
                                   shape plus a word, achromatic but for one
                                   amber — and the two stylesheets hold it by
                                   imitation, so a change to it means editing
                                   both. Presentational: four props, no hook, no
                                   fetch. It takes four of `useBackendHealth()`'s
                                   five fields rather than the result object
                                   whole, and renders NO request id and NO
                                   `version`. Rendered in the chrome since Task
                                   1.12.5, which is what put its stylesheet into
                                   the artefact for the first time

                                   sentence saying which epic fills it, slot —
                                   and since Task 1.7.6 a boundary around that
                                   slot, which is what moved it here from
                                   src/routes/. `useId()` here is the first
                                   hook in the application
      ErrorBoundary/               the codebase's ONLY class component. Keeps a
                                   boolean and not the error, so the fallback

      stories.module.css           workshop furniture: the permutation grid.

packages/
  shared/                          @marketpulse/shared — domain types shared by
                                   backend and frontend; builds to dist/ and is
                                   consumed as a TypeScript project reference
    src/api-error.ts               the wire contract for failures (Task 1.7.3):
                                   ApiError, API_ERROR_CODES and the apiError()
                                   constructor whose branch IS the
                                   exactOptionalPropertyTypes lesson. Task 1.7.4
                                   added BAD_REQUEST and is the one constructor
                                   call site — apps/backend/src/errors.ts. Task
                                   1.12.2 added isApiError() beside them, for
                                   the reason isHealthResponse() sits beside
                                   HealthResponse: the first READER of this
                                   contract arrived four tasks after the
                                   contract did, and a validator written at the
                                   call site is the copy that drifts. It DOES
                                   check `code` against API_ERROR_CODES where
                                   isHealthResponse accepts an unknown `status`
                                   — a discriminator a caller switches on is not
                                   a value the interface renders
    src/request-id.ts              REQUEST_ID_HEADER and nothing else. The name
                                   both apps must import rather than spell
    src/health.ts                  the /health wire contract (Task 1.12.1):
                                   HEALTH_STATUSES, HealthStatus,
                                   HealthResponse and isHealthResponse(). The
                                   predicate is here rather than in the client
                                   because a validator written anywhere but
                                   beside its shape drifts from it — and because
                                   BackendStatus's `degraded` is DEFINED as this
                                   returning false on a body that arrived
    src/backend-status.ts          what a CLIENT concludes, which is a different
                                   fact from what the server reported (Task
                                   1.12.1): BACKEND_STATUSES —
                                   healthy/degraded/unreachable — and
                                   BACKEND_DEGRADED_CAUSES. Deliberately NOT a
                                   widening of HealthStatus: "unreachable" is
                                   the absence of a response, which no server
                                   can report about itself. A test asserts the
                                   two unions share no member
    src/*.test.ts                  tests, beside the subject they test (Task
                                   1.9.2). Inside `include`, so they typecheck
                                   and lint like source — which is why they
                                   also emit into dist/, unreachably
    vitest.config.ts               the runner, scoped to src/**/*.test.ts,
                                   plus the coverage block (Task 1.9.5) scoped
                                   to src/**/*.ts. One per package; there is no
                                   root config, and apps/backend's is the same
                                   shape
scripts/
  check-stories.mjs                the `stories` step of `pnpm verify`. The
                                   first plain-JavaScript file in the workspace,
                                   and the reason eslint.config.mjs has a
                                   globals block for `scripts/`
  check-env-example.mjs            the `env:check` step (Task 1.6.6). Walks
                                   CONFIG_VARIABLES from the backend's BUILT
                                   dist/config.js — the table is TypeScript and
                                   this is not — and checks both .env.example
                                   files against it. Four failure modes, all of
                                   them exercised
  build-image.mjs                  `pnpm image` (Task 1.11.3). NOT a verify step
                                   and it pushes nothing. Exists so the three
                                   load-bearing build arguments cannot be
                                   forgotten, and so a commit-SHA tag means the
                                   tree IS that commit — a dirty tree gets a
                                   `-dirty` suffix rather than a refusal. Moving
                                   it out of package.json also put it inside
                                   ESLint's and Prettier's net, which the
                                   Dockerfile and .dockerignore are still outside
  check-ready.mjs                  `pnpm ready` (Task 1.8.4). NOT a verify step:
                                   verify runs with no servers up. Judges the
                                   frontend on content type, because Vite's dev
                                   server never 404s. Since Task 1.13.2 the two
                                   addresses come from pair-addresses.mjs rather
                                   than from this file; the database's address
                                   comes from that same built config since Task
                                   2.1.3 rather than from a literal. THREE
                                   checks since Task
                                   2.1.2, and the third is not a `fetch` — a
                                   Postgres port answers HTTP by waiting, so it
                                   sends an SSLRequest and reads the one-byte
                                   reply, which is what tells a real server
                                   apart from a mere listener. It REPORTS the
                                   database and does not gate on it (`○`, exit
                                   code unchanged), because nothing opens a
                                   connection yet and `pnpm e2e` gates on this
                                   script. The stated trigger for that becoming
                                   a `✗` is a CONDITION rather than a task
                                   number — the first check in `pnpm verify` or
                                   `pnpm e2e` that fails without a database,
                                   which is Story 2.2's migrations or Story
                                   2.8's routes and NOT the pool
  local-database.mjs               `pnpm db` (Task 2.1.2), and since Task 2.1.3
                                   a READER of the definition rather than the
                                   definition itself. The address, the
                                   credentials and the database name were
                                   literals here until CONFIG_VARIABLES gained
                                   DATABASE_*; keeping them would have been the
                                   second copy pair-addresses.mjs exists to
                                   prevent. It now reads apps/backend's BUILT
                                   dist/config.js, so `pnpm db` needs a built
                                   tree and says so. The direction is the right
                                   way round: POSTGRES_USER/PASSWORD/DB CREATE a
                                   database and DATABASE_* CONNECT to one, so
                                   the creation follows the connection. The
                                   image MAJOR is the one value that stays here,
                                   because nothing in an application's
                                   configuration should name a Postgres version.
                                   Refuses under DATABASE_AUTH=entra rather than
                                   inventing a password. The password is a
                                   fixture and is in the repository on purpose.
                                   `pnpm db` with no arguments is
                                   `up --detach --wait`, which gates on the
                                   healthcheck rather than on the container
                                   existing — that is what lets the readiness
                                   check probe once instead of polling.
                                   Arguments are otherwise forwarded untouched
  pair-addresses.mjs               where the pair IS, defined once (Task
                                   1.13.2). Reads the backend's PORT/HOST from
                                   its BUILT dist/config.js, and the frontend's
                                   origin from CORS_ORIGIN rather than a second
                                   copy of 5173. It moved out of check-ready.mjs
                                   because the browser suite needs the same two
                                   addresses, and a harness with its own copy of
                                   5173 has forked the pair's definition on day
                                   one. Two readers: check-ready.mjs and
                                   run-e2e.mjs
  check-deployed.mjs               is the DEPLOYED pair up, and is the frontend's
                                   artefact coherent right now? (Task 1.13.5.)
                                   `check-ready.mjs`'s counterpart for the live
                                   environment and a separate file, not a
                                   widened copy: it judges two independently
                                   addressed live services. Polls for
                                   COHERENCE rather than a status code — the
                                   document plus every hashed asset it names,
                                   served together — because the frontend's
                                   upload is not atomic. Also carries the
                                   TWO-HOST CONTROL: both halves failing at once
                                   is called out as more likely to be this
                                   machine's link than a simultaneous outage of
                                   two Azure services in two regions, which is
                                   Task 1.11.7's 65-second laptop "outage" turned
                                   into a printed diagnosis
  run-deployed-check.mjs           `pnpm e2e:deployed` (Task 1.13.5). Requires
                                   both deployed addresses and defaults NEITHER,
                                   because deployed they are independent values
                                   that can disagree. Gates on
                                   check-deployed.mjs and propagates the exit
                                   code
  run-e2e.mjs                      `pnpm e2e` (Task 1.13.2). NOT a verify step,
                                   for the reason `ready` is not. Does three
                                   things: resolves the frontend's origin and
                                   passes it as E2E_BASE_URL (playwright.config
                                   .ts has NO default and throws without it);
                                   gates on check-ready.mjs ITSELF rather than a
                                   copy, because a busy 3000 leaves the pair
                                   looking healthy and a one-URL probe passes
                                   against half a system; and propagates the
                                   runner's exit code, signal case included. It
                                   deliberately does NOT start the servers
e2e/                               @marketpulse/e2e — the browser suite (Task
                                   1.13.2). The FOURTH workspace package, at the
                                   root because it is neither an app nor a
                                   library, matched by a literal `e2e` glob.
                                   Three wirings and the third has no symptom:
                                   the glob, `{ "path": "e2e" }` in the root
                                   solution file, and nothing at all for ESLint
                                   or Prettier, which reach it for free
  README.md                        where the RULES live (Task 1.13.3), because
                                   a task file is not where the next person
                                   writing a spec looks: the must-not-assert
                                   list, why no spec stops the backend, the axe
                                   decision, how waits are derived, and what a
                                   green run does NOT certify. package.json's
                                   `description` points at it, because a
                                   manifest cannot hold a comment
  package.json                     lint / typecheck / clean, and NO `test`.

                                   browser suite out of `pnpm test`, which is
                                   "Scope: 4 of 5 workspace projects" — and
                                   nothing checks that it stays absent. Its one
                                   real dependency is axe-core, pinned 4.13.0 to
                                   MATCH the version @storybook/addon-a11y
                                   resolves — two axe versions would make the
                                   workshop and this suite disagree about one
                                   page. Nothing checks that pair
  tsconfig.json                    noEmit + composite, `lib` with DOM and
                                   `types: ["node"]` AT ONCE — a spec runs in

                                   1.13.5). A second CONFIG rather than a second

                                   must never need a local pair. `workers: 1` is

                                   so this check's cost on production is one
                                   countable sequence. outputDir is NESTED
                                   inside test-results/ so the one gitignore
                                   entry covers both
  specs-deployed/                  the deployed journeys (Task 1.13.5). Ten
                                   tests. two-halves.spec.ts catches the two
                                   failures no other instrument here can see —
                                   and by TWO assertions, because they look
                                   identical on screen: a wrong CORS_ORIGIN by
                                   the `healthy` assertion, a wrong
                                   VITE_API_BASE_URL at the CAUSE, by asserting
                                   which origin the page's own request went to.

                                   1.5's deep-link and missing-asset criteria,

                                   cannot hold. Nothing here intercepts a route:
                                   everything asserted is a real environment

  playwright.config.ts             the decisions the first test settles for

                                   default. expect timeout 10 s, not the default
                                   5 s, because 5 s IS the request deadline and
                                   a timeout picked from the healthy figure is

                                   omitted, because it is frozen on macOS 14 and
                                   newPage() never returns

                                   to produce one; backend-recovery is the only
                                   slow test in the repository that is slow for
                                   a reason nothing can shorten — two real poll
                                   intervals, ~62 s
  support/deployed.ts              the deployed environment's two addresses (Task
                                   1.13.5), as two INPUTS and never one input
                                   and a derivation. pair.ts resolves the
                                   frontend's origin FROM CORS_ORIGIN, which is
                                   right locally and is why a wrong allowlist
                                   cannot be reached through `pnpm e2e` at all.
                                   Deployed there are THREE independent values —
                                   VITE_API_BASE_URL in deploy.yml, the Static
                                   Web App's hostname, and CORS_ORIGIN on the
                                   Container App — that all have to agree and
                                   that no file compares. Deriving one from

                                   happy accident in the one place the accident
                                   is the bug
  support/                         locators, the two poll constants and the axe
                                   pass. Inside the tsconfig and OUTSIDE

                                   numbers cannot be imported (every path to
                                   them runs through api-base-url.ts, which
                                   reads import.meta.env at module load and
                                   throws under Node), so backend-recovery
                                   measures the interval the running application
                                   actually polls at and asserts it against the
                                   copy
planning/
  PRODUCT_SPEC.md                  authoritative product definition
  EPICS.md                         epic roadmap and delivery sequence
  epic-NN-<slug>/
    EPIC.md                        goal, scope, exit criteria, story index
    story-NN-<slug>/
      STORY.md                     description, acceptance criteria,
                                   open decisions; tasks live alongside it
```

Work descends epic → story → task. Read the STORY.md before starting on a story: several carry **open decisions** (framework, UI library, hosting) that are deliberately unresolved and should be settled with the user rather than assumed.

We are building this in **small iterations**. Do not scaffold ahead of the current step: build the thin slice that is asked for, keep it working, then move on. Do not add infrastructure (databases, workers, WebSockets, agent plumbing) before the iteration that needs it.

Keep this section, and the Commands section, updated as things actually land.

## Commands

```
corepack enable    # once per machine — pnpm comes from the repo pin, not a global install
pnpm install

pnpm verify        # build → lint → format:check → stories → env:check → test → test:process.
                   # What CI runs, by name (Task 1.10.2). Seven steps since Task 1.10.5

pnpm build         # tsc -b, THEN the frontend bundle, THEN Storybook — see below
pnpm typecheck     # the tsc -b half only; no bundle
pnpm lint          # eslint . over the whole workspace in one process; also lint:fix
pnpm stories       # fails if a component has no stories file
pnpm env:check     # fails if .env.example and CONFIG_VARIABLES have drifted apart
pnpm db            # the LOCAL development database (Task 2.1.2). PostgreSQL 18 in a
                   # container, `compose.yaml` + scripts/local-database.mjs. Since Task
                   # 2.1.3 it needs a BUILT tree, because where the database is comes out
                   # of apps/backend/dist/config.js — so `DATABASE_PORT=5433` in
                   # apps/backend/.env moves the container, the client and `pnpm ready`'s
                   # probe in one edit. NOT part of
                   # verify and NOT part of `pnpm dev`: a database is a fourth process
                   # with a different lifecycle — it holds state and must survive Ctrl-C.
                   # With no arguments it is `docker compose up --detach --wait`, so it
                   # does not return until the server is accepting connections; arguments
                   # are otherwise forwarded, so `pnpm db down`, `pnpm db logs -f`,
                   # `pnpm db ps` and `pnpm db exec postgres psql …` all work. Data
                   # survives `down`; `down -v` removes it. Docker is a prerequisite for
                   # THIS and nothing else — install, verify, dev and e2e all run without
                   # it. A bare `docker compose up` deliberately FAILS naming this script
pnpm ready         # is the running pair actually up? NOT part of verify — see below.
                   # THREE checks since Task 2.1.2: the database is REPORTED (`○`) and
                   # does not change the exit code, because nothing opens a connection
                   # yet and `pnpm e2e` gates on this script. What flips it is a
                   # CONDITION rather than a task: the first check that fails without one
pnpm image         # builds the backend's linux/amd64 container image. NOT part of verify,
                   # and pushes nothing (Task 1.11.2). Since Task 1.11.3 it is
                   # scripts/build-image.mjs rather than a one-liner: a clean tree gets the
                   # bare short SHA, a DIRTY tree gets `<sha>-dirty` and a warning not to push
                   # it, and MARKETPULSE_IMAGE_TAG overrides both (Task 1.11.6's door)
pnpm test          # real in all three packages — 229 tests (37 + 89 + 103). Fast: no build, no socket
pnpm test:process  # the backend's process half — 14 tests spawning dist/index.js (Tasks 1.10.5, 2.1.4).
                   # A verify step in its own right; ~7.6 s, of which 5 s IS the shutdown ceiling
pnpm e2e           # the browser suite (Tasks 1.13.2-3). Playwright, Chromium, against a pair
                   # YOU started with `pnpm dev` — it gates on `pnpm ready` and does not
                   # start the servers. NOT a verify step, for the reason `ready` is not.
                   # Drives the origin CORS_ORIGIN names, resolved from the running
                   # backend's own config; arguments are forwarded, so --headed, --debug
                   # and -g all work. The browsers are a separate explicit command:
                   # `pnpm exec playwright install chromium` (~554 MB, once per machine).
                   # Ten journeys in ~1:02 wall, of which NINE take 3.4 s and the tenth
                   # is the recovery journey waiting out two real 30 s poll intervals.
                   # `pnpm e2e --grep-invert "recovers on the next poll"` is the fast half.
                   # Read e2e/README.md before writing a spec.
                   # CI runs it as the `e2e` JOB in verify.yml — a second job, in
                   # parallel with the chain, starting `pnpm dev` itself, and a
                   # REQUIRED CHECK on `main` since Task 1.13.4
pnpm e2e:deployed  # the SAME browser against the LIVE environment (Task 1.13.5). Needs
                   # E2E_DEPLOYED_BASE_URL and E2E_DEPLOYED_BACKEND_ORIGIN and defaults
                   # NEITHER — deployed they are independent values that can disagree,
                   # which is the failure it exists to catch. Gates on
                   # scripts/check-deployed.mjs, which polls until the document and every
                   # hashed asset it names are served TOGETHER, because the frontend's
                   # upload is not atomic and its window opens at the second the deploy
                   # step reports success. Ten tests, 10.5 s green. NOT a verify step and
                   # NOT in the ruleset: it runs after a merge, so its output is a
                   # rollback decision rather than a gate. CI runs it as the
                   # `check-deployed` JOB in deploy.yml, `needs: deploy`. No schedule,
                   # deliberately — that would make it uptime monitoring
pnpm coverage      # the same tests with --coverage; NOT part of test or verify.
                   # CI runs it as a separate, non-gating step (Task 1.10.4)
pnpm dev           # per-package, in parallel; all three are real loops
pnpm clean         # tsc -b --clean, plus the frontend's dist/ and storybook-static/
pnpm format        # prettier --write .  — the whole tree
pnpm format:check  # prettier --check .

# Working on one package — the same six verbs, meaning the same thing:
pnpm --filter @marketpulse/shared build       # or typecheck / lint / lint:fix / test
pnpm --filter @marketpulse/shared test        # vitest run — 37 tests, 4 files
pnpm --filter @marketpulse/backend test       # vitest run — 89 tests, 6 files
pnpm --filter @marketpulse/backend run test:process   # vitest run --config vitest.process.config.ts — 14 tests
pnpm --filter @marketpulse/frontend test      # vitest run — 103 tests, 12 files
pnpm --filter @marketpulse/backend coverage   # one package's report

# Running less than everything (Task 1.9.6). The path is relative to the
# PACKAGE, not the repository root, and nothing needs a `--` — see below.
pnpm --filter @marketpulse/backend test src/config.test.ts     # one file
pnpm --filter @marketpulse/frontend test PriceChange           # any path substring
pnpm --filter @marketpulse/backend test -t "freezes what it returns"   # one test by name
pnpm --filter @marketpulse/shared test --reporter=verbose      # list the names to match
pnpm --filter @marketpulse/frontend exec vitest --watch        # watch mode; --watch is REQUIRED
pnpm --filter @marketpulse/shared dev         # tsc -b --watch
pnpm --filter @marketpulse/shared run clean   # `run` is required here — see below

pnpm --filter @marketpulse/backend dev        # tsc -b --watch + node --watch dist/index.js
pnpm --filter @marketpulse/backend start      # node dist/index.js — runs the built output; build first

pnpm --filter @marketpulse/frontend dev       # vite — http://localhost:5173
pnpm --filter @marketpulse/frontend preview   # vite preview — serves dist/ on :4173; build first
pnpm --filter @marketpulse/frontend storybook # the workshop — http://localhost:6006
pnpm --filter @marketpulse/frontend storybook:build  # static build into storybook-static/
```

Every package exposes `dev`, `build`, `test`, `lint`, `typecheck`, `clean` and they mean the same thing in each. `lint:fix` is an extra, not part of the convention — a local convenience with no root fan-out and no place in `verify`. **`apps/backend`'s `start` (Task 1.2.5), `apps/frontend`'s `preview` (Task 1.3.4) and `apps/frontend`'s `storybook` and `storybook:build` (Task 1.4.5) all have exactly the same status**: an extra, not a seventh verb — no root fan-out, no place in `verify`, and no obligation on the other packages. Both run the built output and build nothing themselves, so a stale or empty `dist/` is a stale or missing server and a stale page. Verified in Task 1.2.5 and again in 1.2.6: `pnpm run` waits for the script to finish and propagates both signals and the child's exit code, so `start` is a real process wrapper rather than a fire-and-forget one. `SIGTERM` to the pnpm process and `SIGINT` to the process group both give a clean exit 0 with the port released; a busy port gives exit 1 with the `EADDRINUSE` record intact. `test` was an `echo` placeholder in all three packages and the **only** placeholder left in the workspace; Tasks 1.9.2, 1.9.3 and 1.9.4 made all three real (`vitest run` in each). **There is no placeholder script anywhere in this workspace now.** All three `dev` scripts have been real since Task 1.3.3. **`coverage` (Task 1.9.5) is the first script to sit between those two categories**: it is in all three packages and it does fan out from the root, like the six verbs, but it is not part of the convention and not in `verify` — a package added tomorrow owes `test`, not `coverage`. It is the same `vitest run` with `--coverage` appended, so it cannot disagree with `test` about which files are tests.

**Storybook stays out of `pnpm dev`, and Task 1.8.2 confirmed it on a measurement rather than leaving it as an omission.** The suspicion was that its boxed startup banner would be mangled by pnpm's line prefix; it is not — the box survives the prefix intact, at **12 startup lines** against Vite's 3, and the workshop is as quiet as Vite afterwards. What decides it is the port: **Storybook does not strict-port.** **Task 1.8.7 re-measured this and refined it — it does not silently bind 6007, it _asks_.** With 6006 held on both address families, Storybook 10.5.10 prints `Port 6006 is not available. / Would you like to run Storybook on port 6007 instead?` with a Yes/No selector **defaulting to Yes**; on a pty that is an interactive prompt, and **with no tty it prints the prompt and waits indefinitely, binding nothing** (observed for 45 s). So inside `pnpm dev`'s non-interactive fan-out the outcome is a **hang**, not a moved port — 1.8.2's "bound 6007 and said so in a line nobody reads" is that prompt's default. Either way it is the behaviour `strictPort: true` was chosen to prevent for 5173 and 4173, and the decision is unchanged and its argument stronger: a loop that hangs waiting for an answer nobody can give is worse than one that moves a port. Note the squatter has to hold **both** families — an IPv4-only squatter on 6006 does not conflict at all, because Storybook binds IPv6 loopback like Vite does, and the run then looks like a clean start. Beside that, the workshop and the application are different activities, so running both always leaves one bundler idling. The reversal trigger is a story being edited routinely in the same sitting as the application, and the port question has to be answered first.

**`vite preview` is not a static host, and mistaking it for one hides a real failure.** ~~Its SPA fallback answers _any_ unmatched path with `index.html` and a 200~~ — **the word _any_ is wrong and Task 1.12.6 found it by pointing `VITE_API_BASE_URL` at this server (2026-09-04): the fallback keys on the `Accept` header.** With `Accept: text/html`, or with no `Accept` at all, `/health` and a made-up path both come back 200 `index.html`; with **`Accept: application/json` both come back 404** — and `application/json` is exactly what `apps/frontend/src/api-client.ts` sends, so an API client pointed here reads a 404 where a browser reads a page. The original finding is unchanged and is about a browser: `/assets/nope.js` still arrives as a MIME-type error rather than a 404 naming the file. Measured against `python3 -m http.server`, which 404s both. **Vite 8's preview server is also CORS-permissive** — it reflects the request `Origin` in `Access-Control-Allow-Origin`, verified on both the 200 and the 404 — so a cross-origin browser client reads that 404 normally rather than being refused. Use `preview` to look at a build; use something dumb to prove one works. **And do not carry that `Accept` behaviour to the deployed host, because Task 1.12.7 measured the opposite (2026-09-04): Azure Static Web Apps' `navigationFallback` is a URL-pattern rule and not an `Accept` rule.** `/health` there answers **200 `index.html` in identical bytes at both `Accept: application/json` and `Accept: text/html`**, while `/assets/health` 404s at both — so the two hosts disagree about the one request an API client actually sends, and a client pointed at `vite preview` reads `degraded` / `not-ok-status` where the same client pointed at the deployed frontend reads `degraded` / `unreadable-body`. Check the header before attributing a state to the host. **The sweep-safe form of this, because the phrase rots rather than the number: three hosts, three behaviours — `python3 -m http.server` 404s both, `vite preview` splits on `Accept`, Azure Static Web Apps splits on path — so never write "the SPA fallback" without saying which host you mean.**

**Now that there is a router, that generosity hides two acceptance criteria rather than one.** Task 1.5.5 served the built artefact from outside the workspace with `python3 -m http.server`: `/` is a 200 and `/investigations`, `/securities`, `/replay` and any made-up path are all **404**, while `vite preview` and the dev server answer every one of them — and `/assets/nope.js` — with the same 1101-byte `index.html`. So **deep-linking is a property of the host and not of the router**, and it was Story 1.11's to configure; hash routing was rejected as `/#/` permanently in every URL a user copies. **Task 1.11.4 configured it and closed both criteria against the deployed site (2026-09-03)** — `navigationFallback` with `exclude: ["/assets/*"]`, every route a **200 with `index.html` and not a redirect**, a made-up path rendering `NotFound` in a browser, and `/assets/nope.js` a **404**. The half that is easy to miss: **the not-found route rests on the same property.** `NotFound` is a real route, so it only renders if the host served `index.html` for the address that matched nothing — on a dumb host the user gets _Python's_ 404 page and React never boots. Do not record either criterion as met on local evidence. Three constraints are written into Story 1.11's STORY.md, and the non-obvious one is that the rewrite must **not** be a blanket catch-all, because one that answers every unmatched path with `index.html` recreates the missing-asset trap above in production.

**`clean` is the one verb that needs an explicit `run` when filtered, and this is a trap with teeth.** `pnpm clean` is a _built-in pnpm 11 command_ (alias `purge`) that removes `node_modules` from every workspace project. pnpm runs a `clean` script instead of the built-in only when the current project has one — which the root does, so root `pnpm clean` correctly runs `tsc -b --clean`. But `pnpm --filter <pkg> clean` dispatches to the built-in and fails with `[ERROR] Unknown option: 'recursive'` (exit 1), because `--filter` implies `--recursive` and the built-in takes no such flag. Verified in Task 1.2.6 for all three packages; the documented one-liner used to list `clean` alongside the other verbs and was simply wrong. **`pnpm --filter <pkg> run clean` works.** The failure is loud and deletes nothing, but do not "fix" it by dropping the root's `clean` script, which is the only thing shadowing a command that deletes `node_modules`.

**A green `pnpm test` finally means something, and it means exactly this: ~~118~~ ~~137~~ ~~160~~ ~~170~~ ~~183~~ ~~189~~ ~~207~~ ~~218~~ 229 tests pass.** `packages/shared` runs **37 across 4 files** — it was 7 across 2 until Task 1.12.1 moved the health contract here, and 26 until Task 1.12.2 added `isApiError` beside the shape it checks — `apps/backend` **89 across 6** — it was 49 across 3 until Task 2.1.4 added the pool's eleven, 67 across 4 until Task 2.1.6 added `entra-token.test.ts`'s ten and one more on the pool, and 78 across 5 until Task 2.1.7 added the diagnostic route's six and the cached check's five — and `apps/frontend` **103 across 12** (Task 1.11.5 added 15 over the API base URL and the health probe; Task 1.12.2 re-homed most of those onto the client and took the package to 74; Task 1.12.3 deleted the probe's four and added 14 for the polling hook, taking it to 84; Task 1.12.4's status indicator added 13, taking it to 97; Task 1.12.5's wiring added 6 — two on the header, four on `App` — taking it to 103), and each exits 1 when one fails — demonstrated by breaking one in each package rather than reasoned about, with the root exit code 1 every time. The "a green tick means no tests exist" warning that stood in three places through Task 1.9.3 is gone from all three in this same change — here, in `README.md` and in ADR 0001 §5. Story 1.9 planned that removal for Task 1.9.7 to avoid an inconsistent set between tasks; the three became false **simultaneously**, the moment the last placeholder went, so removing them together here _is_ the one change, and leaving two false warnings standing for three more tasks would be the exact failure the triplication was set up to avoid. What a green tick still does **not** mean is coverage — `pnpm coverage` is the separate command that measures it. And `pnpm test` still does not reach the backend's process half at all: that is `pnpm test:process`, ten more tests under a second runner in the same package (Task 1.10.5), and both are steps in `pnpm verify`. **Task 1.9.7 checked that nothing reintroduced the warning and found the count was wrong in the other direction: it stood in thirteen places, not three.** The eleven verbatim "Conventions from Story 1.1" blocks in Epic 1's story files carried it, as did `EPIC.md` itself and Story 1.10's own notes — all of them amended in one change, with the original struck through rather than deleted, exactly as the `dev`-placeholder correction was in Task 1.3.3. The transferable half: **a sentence duplicated for legibility has to be counted with a grep before it can be corrected**, and the count that was written down was of the places somebody remembered. **Task 1.10.8 ran the same sweep and it caught the same class of drift one story later.** The eleven blocks are not eleven copies of one sentence: nine are byte-identical, and Stories 1.2 and 1.3 carry their own historical strike-throughs, so a hash comparison is the way to read them rather than a diff. What the comparison found is that **eight of them still said the backend's process half is unreachable by any runner** — false since Task 1.10.5, and amended only in Story 1.10's own copy at the time. All eight are amended here in one change, struck through rather than deleted. The rule the sweep exists for is now proven twice: **an amendment made in the copy you are editing is not an amendment to the convention.**
**Running one file or one test needs no `--`, and the plausible form that adds one is silently wrong (Task 1.9.6).** `pnpm --filter @marketpulse/backend test src/config.test.ts` is 1 file / 16 tests and `pnpm --filter @marketpulse/backend test -t "freezes what it returns"` is 1 passed / 48 skipped; both work identically from the package directory as `pnpm test <arg>`, and `pnpm t` is the same command again. Every form above was executed. Four things about it that were measured rather than assumed. **`test` is a pnpm built-in** — `t, test` in `pnpm help -a`, "runs a package's `test` script" — but unlike `clean` the built-in and the script do the same thing, so there is no collision and no `run` is needed; `--filter` and trailing arguments both reach `vitest` untouched, `--reporter=verbose` included, even though `--reporter` is also one of pnpm's own flags. **The path is relative to the package**, so `pnpm --filter @marketpulse/backend test apps/backend/src/config.test.ts` finds nothing — loudly, exit 1. **A bare substring is a path filter**, so `pnpm --filter @marketpulse/frontend test PriceChange` is the ergonomic form and matters most in `apps/frontend`, where a test sits under `src/components/<Name>/` and the path copied from a `packages/shared` example does not exist. And **root `pnpm test <path>` is not the way to narrow**: it is `pnpm -r run test`, so the path goes to all three packages and the two that lack it fail. Use `--filter`.

**Two of those forms fail green, and that is the part to remember.** A **non-matching `-t`** reports "8 skipped | 47 skipped" and exits **0** — a typo in a test name looks like a pass. **`pnpm test -- -t "name"`** is worse: pnpm forwards the `--` literally, Vitest ignores the filter, all 49 tests run and it exits **0**, so the command reads as a successful narrow run and is a full one. Only a **non-matching path** is loud (`No test files found`, exit 1). The rule: after a `-t` run, read the skipped count, not the exit code. Related, and the same class of trap on the coverage side: `pnpm --filter @marketpulse/shared coverage src/api-error.test.ts` reports **20% against the full run's 30%**, because `coverage.include` fixes the denominator while the numerator shrinks — a single-file coverage number is not that file's coverage.

**Watch mode needs an explicit `--watch`, and Vitest 4 changed what a bare `vitest` does.** `pnpm --filter @marketpulse/frontend exec vitest --watch` prints a **`DEV`** banner, `PASS Waiting for file changes...` and `press h to show help, press q to quit`, and touching `src/api-error.test.ts` gives `RERUN src/api-error.test.ts x1` re-running that file alone. A **bare `vitest` does not watch** — it prints the same `RUN` banner as `vitest run`, executes once and exits 0, verified under a real pseudo-terminal both through `pnpm exec` and by calling `node_modules/.bin/vitest` directly, so it is the runner's behaviour and not pnpm's. Anyone carrying the Vitest 3 habit will think watch mode is broken. There is deliberately no `test:watch` script: it would be a seventh verb in three packages for something `exec` already spells, and the six-verb convention is worth more than the keystrokes.

**The test conventions are eight rules, and most of them are forced rather than preferred (Task 1.9.6).** **1. Tests live in `src/` beside their subject as `<subject>.test.ts`** — `.tsx` where they render JSX — because ESLint's project service only ever discovers a `tsconfig.json`, so a test outside the package's `include` is a hard `was not found by the project service` parsing error and loses the whole type-aware pass. The alternative was built and rejected: a `tsconfig.test.json` with `noEmit` typechecks correctly and fails `pnpm lint` on every test file. **2. Configuration is one `vitest.config.ts` per package and there is no root config**, because root `test` is the `pnpm -r` fan-out and a root `projects` list would be a second entry point meaning "run the tests". **Task 1.10.5 added the one exception and it is still not a root config**: `apps/backend` has a second file, `vitest.process.config.ts`, because the process suite is a different _cost_ rather than a different subject — the two configs partition `src/**/*.test.ts` between them and are read as one decision. This is the one place the testing stack departs from `eslint.config.mjs` and `prettier.config.mjs` being single root files, and the reason is that those tools run once from the root and this one does not. **3. Each config scopes `test.include` to `src`**, because Vitest 4's `defaultExclude` is only `node_modules` and `.git` and two packages emit a compiled copy of every test into `dist/`. **The glob is not identical across the three** — `apps/frontend`'s admits `.tsx` or every component test is silently skipped. **4. `apps/frontend` needs `setupFiles` calling `afterEach(cleanup)` and the other two do not**: `@testing-library/react` registers that itself only when it can see a global `afterEach`, and `globals` is off. The two conventions collide silently and the symptom lands in a _later_ test as `getByRole` throwing "found multiple elements". **5. `globals` is off and `describe`/`it`/`expect` are imported by name**, which is what keeps every package's tsconfig `types` array untouched — and `apps/frontend`'s explicit array is the browser boundary's last stated guarantee. **6. A test helper is a module in `apps/frontend` and file-local everywhere else**, and the line is whether the package emits: a `src/test-support.ts` in `packages/shared` or `apps/backend` would ship into `dist/` beside the application, while `apps/frontend` is `noEmit` and so gets `src/test-render.tsx` as a real module — the one description of the application's context for tests, and where every provider Epic 2 adds lands. **7. A `.test.tsx` under `src/components/` owes no stories**, which `scripts/check-stories.mjs` encodes; that narrows what the check reads, not what it demands. **8. Coverage is configured in the same per-package config and never at the root**, with `coverage.include` explicit and scoped to `src` in all three — see the paragraphs below for why the default is the trap. Relative imports in a test carry the `.js` extension like every other file here.

**How to get a server, and why the helper is `async` from its first line.** `buildServer({ logLevel: "silent", logFormat: "json", corsOrigin })` — all three fields, since Story 1.8 made `corsOrigin` required — followed by `await app.ready()`. The `await` is required in its own right: plugin registration is deferred, so the routes do not exist until it resolves. Writing the helper `async` before it needs to be also pre-pays ADR 0002 §3's warning that the first `await` inside the factory changes every caller — one edit rather than every edit.

**There are ~~three levels with a runner behind them and a fourth without one~~ FIVE levels now, and `pnpm test` runs three of them (amended 2026-09-04, Story 1.13).** A **unit** test drives a function with a plain argument, and `loadConfig(env)` is the model — it takes the environment as a parameter specifically so no process has to be mutated. An **integration** test drives the real HTTP layer through `app.inject()`, with both error handlers and CORS already registered by the factory. A **component** test renders the real tree through the one helper and asserts on roles and accessible names. The **fourth** is the backend's process half — signals, exit codes, the shutdown ceiling, `EADDRINUSE`, both crash handlers — which needs a real child process against a **built** tree. The **fifth** is a **browser** test: a real Chromium driving the assembled application over HTTP, which is the only level that can see the two halves talking, a host's routing, or a contrast ratio. `pnpm e2e` points it at a local pair and gates a merge; `pnpm e2e:deployed` points the same tool, the same helpers and the same kind of assertion at the **live** environment after one and gates nothing — a second target rather than a sixth level. What makes the target worth naming separately is that only the deployed one can see a host's `navigationFallback`, a platform-only `CORS_ORIGIN`, or an artefact `verify` never fingerprinted. Neither is in `pnpm test`, and the only thing keeping them out is that `e2e/package.json` has no `test` script. **Task 1.10.5 built it**, as a fourth level with its own runner and its own command (`pnpm test:process`), and the sentence that used to follow this one is now only half true: the coverage table's 0% on `index.ts` is **not** that hole rendered as a figure any more, because a spawned child is invisible to the runner's instrumentation. It is ten tests and 0%. See the process-suite paragraphs below.

**A test can close a gap no `verify` step could, and that is this story's one structural finding.** `apps/backend/src/server.test.ts` walks the registered route table through an `onRoute` hook added by the caller _after_ `buildServer()` returns — which still sees every route, because registration is deferred to `ready()` — and asserts each declares `500: apiErrorSchema`. Nothing in `pnpm verify` could check that, and its first run reported `OPTIONS *`, a preflight route `@fastify/cors` registers and nobody here wrote. **The rule: when the thing being checked is reachable from an assembled instance, a test beats a seventh `verify` step.** Its limit belongs beside it — `setNotFoundHandler` is not a route and can never carry a response schema, so `apiError()`'s four-slot object is what holds there.

**What enforces each convention, and for two of them the answer is "nothing you would notice".** A missing `.js` import extension is `TS2835` from `tsc -b` and **7 passed at exit 0** from `vitest run` — so the runner is a third resolver with the wrong opinion on the negative case, alongside Rolldown, and **a green suite is not evidence the convention was followed**. A test file put outside a tsconfig `include` fails loudly at lint, which is the good case. A component test named `.test.tsx` under a `.ts`-only glob is the bad one and it was measured: a deliberately failing `.test.tsx` dropped into `apps/backend/src/` left `vitest run` reporting 3 files / 49 passed, unchanged — the wholly-empty case is loud (`No test files found`, exit 1) and the partial one is not. A test file added to a package with no `vitest.config.ts`, or with an unscoped `include`, silently runs twice once `dist/` is populated, and it looks like a flaky duplicate rather than a misconfiguration. Type-aware lint, by contrast, is loud on test files and has already had opinions: `non-nullable-type-assertion-style` failed one file twice on the idiom of catching into an `unknown` and casting, answered by narrowing with `instanceof` and then `if (x === undefined) { expect.fail("…") }` — **`expect.fail` returns `never`**, so it narrows as well as fails.

**What a test must not assert, each item traceable to a measurement.** **Not colour** — and since Task 1.9.4 that is structural rather than a discipline: no global stylesheet is applied in the test environment, so `getComputedStyle` returns nothing and `getTokens()` throws. (The desaturation figure still stands separately: the two price directions differ by 1.05:1 in greyscale.) **Not a single element's text where a component splits it** — `getByText("up +12.40")` fails on `PriceChange`, because the direction word is a visually-hidden `<span>` and the figure is a sibling text node; assert the concatenation, which is what a screen reader is handed. **Not a `useId()` value**, only the accessible name it produces — and for the same reason, not a DOM snapshot of a route, since `useId()` output moves when anything above it moves. **Not a boundary reset recovering state that lives _above_ the boundary**: it remounts its children and a parent's state is untouched. **Not a throw escaping `fireEvent` from an event handler** — React dispatches the handler itself and reports the failure to the environment, so `toThrow()` fails while the error separately fails the whole run; a scoped `window` `error` listener calling `preventDefault()` is what makes it assertable. **Not the `.js`-extension convention**, since the runner resolves a bare specifier happily. **Not a CORS rejection through `app.inject()`**, since a string origin makes the server assert the allowed origin unconditionally and the browser is the only enforcer. **Not a 415 from this backend**, since an unparseable content type resolves to a 404 and no request produces one. **Not an axe pass as coverage**, since axe returns `color-contrast` inconclusive on exactly the non-text elements this product encodes with. **And not latency**, unless it is warmed up with a large n and a threshold well outside variance. One reading rule belongs beside these because it is the same class of trap: the coverage command's terminal table lists only the files that are **not** fully covered — `packages/shared`'s shows three of its six — so the table is not the denominator and `<package>/coverage/index.html` is.

**Coverage is one root command, three reports and no threshold (Task 1.9.5).** `pnpm coverage` is `pnpm -r run coverage`, and each package's script is its own `test` with `--coverage` appended — so coverage fans out exactly as `test` does, and the recorded decision that there is **no root Vitest config** stands. Running it once from the root was the alternative and it is not a flag: it means creating the `projects` list Task 1.9.2 declined, with the frontend's entry having to point at a `mergeConfig` over `vite.config.ts` rather than replace it, in exchange for one merged percentage over three packages that share no code. Three tables is the honest shape when the three suites drive three different things. `coverage` was checked against `pnpm help -a` before being claimed and is **not** a pnpm built-in, unlike `clean`, `env`, `config`, `start` and `test`.
