It is **not** in `test` and **not** a seventh `verify` step: nothing gates on the number, and `verify` timings are unchanged at 11.9 s over the same six steps (build 2.11 s, lint 3.67 s, `format:check` 2.80 s, `stories` 0.26 s, `env:check` 0.27 s, `test` 2.51 s). The whole fan-out is **2.8 s** — **2.92 s when Task 1.9.7 re-took it**, with all twelve percentages reproducing to the digit — and the output lands in each package's `coverage/`, which was already in `.gitignore`, `.prettierignore` and `eslint.config.mjs`'s ignores — nothing new needed an entry, confirmed by a clean `git status` after a run.

**The provider is `@vitest/coverage-v8` and `@vitest/coverage-istanbul` was installed and measured before being rejected, and the two are nearly indistinguishable.** Neither ships with the runner. From an identical clean baseline (386 store entries, 265 MB, 4,451 lockfile lines): v8 is **+31 entries, +20 MB, +110 lockfile lines** and istanbul **+29, +20 MB, +94**; neither trips `allowBuilds` and `pnpm-workspace.yaml` is byte-unchanged, so `esbuild` is still the only install script in the tree. Runtime is a wash on the frontend suite (1.87–2.19 s against 1.91–1.94 s, both against 1.58 s uninstrumented). What they report is a wash too: on `packages/shared` the two agree exactly, and on `apps/frontend` they agree on statements, functions and lines to the unit (44/160, 26/59, 42/158 in the run that compared them) and differ by **one branch** — istanbul counts a default parameter (`compact = false` in `ErrorFallback.tsx`, line 90) as a branch and v8 does not. So v8 undercounts branches slightly, and that is the recorded reversal trigger if branch coverage ever gates anything. v8 was taken because it is Vitest's own and does not rewrite the sources it measures, which matters most in `apps/frontend`, whose Vitest config **is** the build config: istanbul's instrumentation would sit inside the same plugin chain that produces `dist/`. It is a root devDependency beside `vitest` rather than a per-package one — no package imports it, the runner resolves it, and all three packages need it, which is the opposite of `jsdom`'s case.

**An explicit `coverage.include` is the whole point, and Vitest 4's defaults are the trap.** `coverageConfigDefaults.exclude` is **`[]`** — read out of the package, not assumed, and the same shape as Task 1.9.2's `defaultExclude` finding — and `coverage.include` is **undefined**, which means "only the files some test loaded". Left at that default, `packages/shared` reports **100% with an empty file table**, because `ticker.ts`, `anomaly.ts` and `feed-status.ts` are simply not in the denominator. Every package therefore sets `include` scoped to `src` (`.ts` in shared and the backend, `.{ts,tsx}` in the frontend, the same `.tsx` trap `test.include` has), which also keeps the provider out of `dist/` — and that matters asymmetrically: `packages/shared` and `apps/backend` emit a compiled copy of every test and every source there, and `apps/frontend` emits nothing at all because it is `noEmit`. Verified rather than assumed: **no `dist/` path and no `*.test.*` file appears in any of the three reports**, and the reports hold 6, 8 and 21 source pages respectively, all of them TypeScript rather than generated output — the backend's `index.ts` page renders 234 lines of the 233-line `.ts` source, comments, `interface` and `readonly` included.

**The first figures, and what they structurally exclude — which is the part worth reading.**

| Package           | Statements            | Branches              | Functions             | Lines                 |
| ----------------- | --------------------- | --------------------- | --------------------- | --------------------- |
| `packages/shared` | ~~30.00%~~ **75.86%** | ~~50.00%~~ **92.00%** | ~~33.33%~~ **71.42%** | ~~30.00%~~ **70.83%** |
| `apps/backend`    | 64.33% (92/143)       | 75.00%                | 72.72%                | 63.82% (90/141)       |
| `apps/frontend`   | ~~68.25%~~ **85.80%** | ~~70.83%~~ **87.50%** | ~~80.64%~~ **88.23%** | ~~67.21%~~ **85.81%** |

**Re-taken in Task 1.12.8 (2026-09-04) and two of the three moved a long way.** Story 1.12 added tests to `packages/shared` and `apps/frontend` and none to `apps/backend`, and the figures say exactly that: shared went 30.00% → **75.86%** and the frontend 68.25% → **85.80%**, while the backend reproduces to the digit. The original figures are struck through rather than replaced, because the paragraphs below are written against them. There is still **no threshold**, for Task 1.10.4's reasons, all of which still hold.

**Two entrypoints are deliberately left in the denominator at 0%**, and that is the single most important configuration decision here. `apps/backend/src/index.ts` is the process — `listen`, both signal handlers, the shutdown ceiling, `EADDRINUSE` and both crash handlers — and `app.inject()` reaches none of it, so the backend's 64% is what it is _because_ the hole Task 1.9.3 handed to Story 1.10 is visible as a figure rather than as a caveat; excluding it would take the package to a flattering number that describes less. `apps/frontend/src/main.tsx` is the same case and is kept for the same reason. What is genuinely outside the report: `scripts/*.mjs`, which no test imports, and `apps/backend/scripts/dev.sh`, which no tool in this workspace reads at all and which this file already records as a `pnpm verify` gap in its own right. The frontend's stories **are** excluded, with the measurement beside the exclusion: statements are **27.04% with them counted against 68.25% without** (159 statements against 63), and the lower number describes the workshop rather than the application — nine story files that nothing executes, since `@storybook/addon-vitest` was measured and rejected in Task 1.9.4. `src/test-render.tsx` and `src/test-setup.ts` are excluded as test scaffolding that is not named `*.test.*`.

**There is no threshold and that is stated rather than omitted.** A minimum over nine components, one route table, one configuration module and no application state would be a number invented before there is anything to hold it to, and it would be met by testing what is easy. **Story 1.10 owns CI and can set one against the table above**, which is what this command exists to produce. One reading note for whoever does: the terminal table lists only files that are **not** fully covered — a file at 100% on all four metrics is omitted from it, so `packages/shared`'s table shows three files out of six (re-counted in Task 1.9.6; the "seven" recorded in Task 1.9.5 was wrong — `src/` holds six non-test modules) — while `<package>/coverage/index.html` lists every file in the denominator.

**The runner is Vitest 4.1.11, installed at the root, and its cost reproduced Task 1.9.1's spike exactly.** `pnpm add -D -w -E vitest@4.1.11` is **+22 packages** (342 → 364 entries in the virtual store), **+4 MB** (246 → 250 MB of `node_modules`) and +223 lines of lockfile, at exit 0. **`allowBuilds` did not fire and `pnpm-workspace.yaml` is byte-unchanged** — a full sweep of the installed tree for `preinstall`/`install`/`postinstall` scripts still returns `esbuild@0.28.2` and nothing else. It is a root devDependency under the standing rule, and `vitest run` resolves from a package directory without that package declaring it. Anything a _test imports_ — a DOM environment, a matcher library — is a dependency of the package that imports it; Task 1.9.4 is where that bites.

**Configuration is one file per package and there is deliberately no root config.** Root `test` is `pnpm -r run test`, one of only two verbs that genuinely fan out, so a root config with a `projects` list would be a second entry point meaning "run the tests" — which Story 1.9 says explicitly not to introduce. `packages/shared/vitest.config.ts` is the first and `apps/backend/vitest.config.ts` (Task 1.9.3) the second, and both are a **`.ts` and not the `.mts`** Task 1.9.1 called for: that finding was about the _root_, whose `package.json` has no `"type": "module"` and which therefore loads a `.ts` config as CommonJS and warns. Every workspace package is ESM, so a `.ts` config there loads as ESM and warns about nothing — verified, zero warnings. They are the **fifth and sixth** files needing `eslint.config.mjs`'s trailing `disableTypeChecked` block, for the identical reason `vite.config.ts` and the two `.storybook/` files are: a `.ts` file in a package whose tsconfig `include` is `src/**/*`. Without the entry it is a hard `was not found by the project service` parsing error, not a silent skip.

**Test files live in `src/` beside their subject, and that is forced rather than chosen.** ESLint's project service only ever discovers a `tsconfig.json`, so a test outside the package's `include` is that same hard parsing error and loses the whole type-aware pass. The alternative was built before it was rejected: a `packages/shared/tsconfig.test.json` with `noEmit`, referencing `./tsconfig.json`, with tests excluded from the main project. It **typechecks correctly** — `tsc -b` on it is exit 0, and a deliberate `const bad: number = REQUEST_ID_HEADER` is TS2322 at exit 2 — and it fails `pnpm lint` on both test files, because nothing points the project service at it and there is no per-package way to. So a test file gets type-aware linting or a clean `dist/`, and this workspace takes the linting. Naming is `<subject>.test.ts` beside `<subject>.ts`; **everything after this copies it**, so Tasks 1.9.3 and 1.9.4 inherit the location rather than re-deciding it.

**The price of that is real and has two halves, one of which is a trap.** `tsc -b` emits `dist/*.test.js`, `.d.ts`, and both maps — **8 files for 2 test files** in `packages/shared`, and 12 for 3 in `apps/backend`, in the directory that _is_ this package's contract. The harmless half: a consumer cannot reach them, measured rather than assumed — `import("@marketpulse/shared/dist/api-error.test.js")` is `ERR_PACKAGE_PATH_NOT_EXPORTED`, because the `exports` map declares `"."` and nothing else — and the frontend artefact is byte-identical with them in the tree (271 modules / 343,658 B / 10,926 B / three files / md5 `cba2825c…`, unchanged since Task 1.7.7). **The trap: `tsc -b --clean` deletes the output of the sources that currently exist, so deleting a test file first orphans its four `dist/` files permanently** — the documented rule now applies to tests too, and a stray `dist/api-error.test.js` is a file the next paragraph explains why you do not want lying around.

**`dist/` is not on Vitest 4's default exclude list, and left alone that runs every test twice.** `defaultExclude` is **`['**/node_modules/**', '**/.git/**']` and nothing more** — read out of the package, not assumed, and it is shorter than Vitest 3's. With `dist/` populated, an unconfigured `vitest run` in `packages/shared` collects **4 files and 14 tests** against the 2 and 7 that exist, and the second copy comes from whatever the last build emitted rather than from the source just edited — so a test edited and re-run shows its old and new selves side by side. `test.include` is scoped to `src/**/*.test.ts`, which takes it back to 2 and 7. **Task 1.9.3 reproduced it exactly in `apps/backend`** — the shipping config collects 3 files and 49 tests, an empty config collects **6 and 98** — and it is worse there than in shared, because a backend test builds a server, so the duplicate is a second `buildServer()` running against whatever `dist/` last held. It is an **allowlist rather than an `exclude` of `dist/`**, for the reason `config.ts` gives for rejecting `redact`: a denylist's failure mode is the entry nobody added. The backend's unit config gained one `exclude` in Task 1.10.5 — `src/**/*.process.test.ts` — and that is the complement of an allowlist rather than a denylist: it is the same glob the process config `include`s, and the alternative (narrowing the unit `include` to `src/*.test.ts`) is the trap Task 1.9.4 measured, where a too-narrow glob silently skips the first test that does not fit it.

**The `.js`-extension convention works in a test file, and Vitest is now the third resolver with an opinion about the negative case — it has the wrong one.** `import { apiError } from "./api-error.js"` in a `.ts` test, in a package with `"type": "module"` and `module: nodenext`, resolves **unconfigured**. Drop the extension and `tsc -b` is **TS2835 at exit 2** while `vitest run` is **7 passed at exit 0** — the same asymmetry ADR 0003 records for Rolldown, arriving in a third place. So `tsc` is still the only enforcer of the convention, and the new consequence is that **a green test suite is not evidence the convention was followed** any more than a green `vite build` is. That is the second half of Task 1.9.1's rule: the runner transpiles and strips types, it does not check them, which is why test files have to sit inside a tsconfig `include` in the first place.

**The backend suite is 49 tests in three files, and the split is by what can be tested rather than by module.** `src/config.test.ts` and `src/request-id.test.ts` test pure functions beside their subjects; **`src/server.test.ts` is one file for what looks like four subjects** — the route, the error contract, the correlation id and CORS — because every one of those is a property of the _assembled instance_: `registerErrorHandling` and `registerCors` take a Fastify instance and return nothing, so there is no unit to test that is not the server. There is deliberately **no `src/test-support.ts`**: test files are forced to live inside the package's tsconfig `include`, so a helper module would emit into `dist/` and be scaffolding shipped beside the server. A file-local helper costs nothing. The helper is **`async` from its first line** even though `buildServer()` is synchronous today — `await app.ready()` is required in its own right, since plugin registration is deferred and the routes do not exist until it resolves, and ADR 0002 §3's warning (the first `await app.register(...)` inside the factory changes every caller) then costs one edit rather than every edit.

**The response-schema gap Story 1.9 was handed is closed by a test, and its first run found a route nobody wrote.** Nothing in `pnpm verify` checks that a route which can fail declared `500: apiErrorSchema`, and forgetting it is silent until something throws. The route table **is** reachable in a form worth asserting on: an `onRoute` hook added by the caller _after_ `buildServer()` returns still sees every route, because `app.register(healthRoutes)` inside the factory has not run yet — registration is deferred to `ready()`. The test walks the table and asserts identity against `apiErrorSchema`. It reported **`OPTIONS *`** on its first run: `@fastify/cors` registers a wildcard preflight handler, which answers 204 with no body, so there is nothing for a schema to strip and requiring one would mean forking the library's route. It is exempted **by its exact signature** rather than by "anything OPTIONS", so a preflight route we ever declare ourselves is still checked. `setNotFoundHandler` is not a route and can never have a schema, so it stays structurally outside this check and `apiError()`'s four-slot object is what holds there — asserted separately.

**Three things about testing this server that the obvious approach gets wrong.** To prove `fast-json-stringify`'s stripping on the **real** route rather than on a copy of its schema, decorate the payload in a **`preSerialization`** hook — an `onSend` hook is handed a string that has already been stripped, and a second route declaring a copy of the schema only proves the copy works. **CORS cannot be made to fail by injection**: with a string origin `@fastify/cors` asserts `access-control-allow-origin` unconditionally, so a test sending an unlisted `Origin` and expecting a rejection fails — the browser is the enforcer. What is assertable is the header's presence and value, `REQUEST_ID_HEADER` in `access-control-expose-headers`, and the preflight `access-control-allow-methods`, which is pinned at **`GET,HEAD,POST`** so the first route taking a `PUT` fails here rather than in a browser. And **`app.inject()` reaches none of `index.ts`** — signals, exit codes, the shutdown ceiling, `EADDRINUSE` and both crash handlers need a real child process against a **built** tree. Task 1.9.3 recorded that whole class as out of scope with **Story 1.10 as its owner** rather than half-building one: a process test needs a build to have run, spawns and signals, and its first failure mode is a port held by a previous run — putting one in the same `vitest run` as 48 injected ones makes the fast suite conditional on a build and occasionally flaky. **Task 1.10.5 honoured that argument rather than reversing it**: the suite exists, and it is a second config and a second command in the same package precisely so the 49 stay fast.

**Type-aware lint runs on test files and it has opinions the suite has to meet.** `@typescript-eslint/non-nullable-type-assertion-style` failed the first draft twice, both times on the idiom of catching an error into an `unknown` and casting it. The answer that needs no cast and no `!`: narrow with `instanceof` in the `catch`, then `if (x === undefined) { expect.fail("…") }` — **`expect.fail` returns `never`**, so it narrows the type as well as failing the test. This is the practical half of Task 1.9.2's rule that a test file is source: it typechecks, it lints under `strictTypeChecked`, and `--max-warnings 0` applies.

**The DOM environment is jsdom 30.0.1, and happy-dom was built and measured before being rejected.** Cost of the choice: **+22 entries in the virtual store** (364 → 386), **+15 MB** (250 → 265 MB) and +340 lockfile lines, for `jsdom` plus `@testing-library/react` and its `@testing-library/dom` peer; `allowBuilds` did not fire and `pnpm-workspace.yaml` is byte-unchanged, so `esbuild` is still the only install script in the tree. Note **Task 1.9.1's spike recorded "+36 packages" and that is pnpm's own `+36 -1` install summary, which counts links into `node_modules` trees rather than unique store entries** — this repository's convention is the store count, so the figure to quote is +22. All three are devDependencies of `apps/frontend` and not of the root: `@testing-library/react` is imported by test code, and `jsdom` is resolved by name from a string by the runner, which is the same shape that made `pino-pretty` a real dependency of `apps/backend`. **No fidelity difference was found**, and that is a measurement rather than a shrug: both environments rendered the Base UI popover through its portal, resolved `useId()`-generated `aria-labelledby` identically, computed the same accessible names, and produced identical hashed CSS Module class names. The one difference found runs the _other_ way — jsdom 30 has no `Element.checkVisibility()` and happy-dom does. happy-dom is also cheaper (+7 packages) and about 3× faster to set up (environment 130 ms against 380–410 ms). jsdom was taken anyway, on the failure mode rather than on a capability: it is the reference implementation every library in this stack is tested against, and a re-implementation's divergences arrive as "the DOM behaved differently from a browser", which is silent and indistinguishable from a component bug — the same argument Task 1.8.3 used to take `@fastify/cors` over a hand-rolled hook. **The reversal trigger is the frontend suite growing to dominate `pnpm test`**, where ~275 ms of setup per run stops being free.

**`mergeConfig(viteConfig, …)` buys real CSS Modules, and it also does not apply a single global stylesheet — both halves matter.** A rendered `PriceChange` carries `class="_change_ea28d5 _positive_ea28d5"`, Vite's own scoped names, not the identity proxy an unconfigured runner hands back. But `getTokens()` **throws** in a test — `Design token --surface-page resolved to nothing` — because `tokens.css`, `market.css` and `base.css` are never injected, so `getComputedStyle` on `:root` returns empty. Two consequences. The rule "do not assert on colour" is not a discipline here, it is a **structural impossibility**, which is a better guarantee than the discipline was. And anything that imports `.storybook/preview.tsx` into a test inherits that throw — which is exactly what happened to `@storybook/addon-vitest` below.

**@testing-library's automatic cleanup does not register, because `globals` is off — and the two decisions collide silently.** RTL registers its own `afterEach(cleanup)` only when it can see a global `afterEach`; `globals: false` is what keeps every package's tsconfig `types` array untouched, and `apps/frontend`'s explicit array is the browser boundary's last stated guarantee. Measured before it was fixed: two tests each rendering one component left `document.body` holding **1 and then 2** children. Nothing fails at that point — it surfaces later, in a third test, as `getByRole` throwing "found multiple elements" in a test that did nothing wrong. `apps/frontend/src/test-setup.ts` is what replaces it, and it is deliberately **not** the render helper: a leaf-component test calls `render()` directly and needs the cleanup just as much.

**Putting a test beside its subject under `src/components/` collided with `pnpm stories`, and the fix narrows what that check reads.** `scripts/check-stories.mjs` treats every non-`.stories.tsx` `.tsx` under that directory as a component owing stories, so `PriceChange.test.tsx` was reported as a component wanting a `PriceChange.test.stories.tsx` and `pnpm stories` exited 1 — measured, not anticipated. It now skips `.test.tsx`. That narrows what the check **reads**, not what it demands: a real component with no stories still fails, which is the one direction this check has ever enforced.

**`App.test.tsx` drives the real `<BrowserRouter>` through `window.history`, and that is the whole reason it is worth having.** `App` contains its own router, so the shared helper's `MemoryRouter` would nest two; and re-declaring the five `<Route>`s inside a `MemoryRouter` would test a **copy** of the route table, which is the thing that can drift from the one that ships. `window.history.pushState()` before `render(<App />)` keeps the subject `App.tsx` itself, `basename` and all. What it closes is the gap `paths.ts` states and does not close — **nothing checks that a declared path has a route** — and it was proved by adding a `breadth: "/breadth"` entry with no `<Route>`, which fails naming the path. Its limit is worth knowing too: a path and its route both read `PATHS`, so a typo that moves both together still passes, and **a pass here is not deep-linking working in production**, which is a property of the host (Story 1.11) and not of the router.

**Three things about testing React here that the obvious approach gets wrong, each found by writing it wrong first.** `getByText("up +12.40")` **fails** on `PriceChange` — the direction word is in its own visually-hidden `<span>` and the figure is a sibling text node, so no single element holds both, and Testing Library says so ("the text is broken up by multiple elements"); assert the concatenation, which is what a screen reader is handed. An `ErrorBoundary` remount test needs the crash flag in the **child**, not in a parent above the boundary: the boundary remounts its children and the parent's state is untouched, so a freshly mounted child throws again on the spot — **a boundary reset recovers the child's own state, not the state of whatever above it caused the failure.** And **a throw from an event handler does not come back out of `fireEvent`** — React dispatches the handler itself and reports the failure to the environment, so `expect(...).toThrow()` fails while the error separately fails the whole run as an unhandled error; a `window` `error` listener calling `preventDefault()`, scoped to the one click, is what makes the product behaviour assertable. That listener is also precisely the thing the application deliberately does not install (Task 1.7.6).

**`@storybook/addon-vitest` was rejected, and the story's predicted blocker did not materialise — so the rejection rests on shape rather than cost.** Story 1.9 expected `@vitest/browser` and a Playwright download. Measured: `pnpm add -D -E @storybook/addon-vitest@10.5.10` is **+1 package**, pulls no Playwright and no `@vitest/browser`, and trips no install script. It then **works under jsdom**, running all 9 story files as **47 smoke tests** — but only with `css: true`, because `.storybook/preview.tsx` calls `getTokens()` and that throws with no stylesheet applied; without it every story file fails to load. `css: true` itself costs nothing measurable on the unit suite (1.22–1.26 s against 1.20–1.48 s). What it costs that is not measurable in seconds: a `projects` array making `apps/frontend` the only package with two test projects, a second source for Task 1.9.5's coverage to reconcile, and — the deciding argument — **the stories are a workshop, not assertions**. `AppHeader`'s `AllPermutations` has two a11y rules disabled on it deliberately, because a grid of six banners is not a page; adopting the addon makes the workshop's visual-review compromises into test fixtures, and "renders without throwing" is the weakest assertion available on ten components that now have written tests. **The reversal trigger is a component shipping without a written test, or Epic 15 wanting axe to fail rather than report** — at which point the cost is +1 package and one `css: true`, both recorded here so nobody re-derives them.

**The browser boundary was re-measured rather than cited, and Task 1.6.4's regression is still there.** A test file using `process` **typechecks at exit 0** — `types: ["vite/client"]` still does not stand there, because `@types/node` reaches the program through a `/// <reference>` in a declaration file the stories drag in. What fires is the lint rule: `no-restricted-globals` over `apps/frontend/src/**` reported it by name. So the boundary is exactly where Task 1.6.4 left it, and a test file under `src/` gets it for free — which is one of the reasons tests live there. **The `types` array was not touched**, and neither was any other package's.

**The React Compiler rules met real state for the first time and said nothing.** They had never fired outside a spike, and the stated reason was that nothing shipped here has state. `ErrorBoundary.test.tsx` contains this tree's first stateful components — a counter with `useState` and two `setState` handlers — and all 17 rules were silent under `--max-warnings 0`. What did fire, twice, is type-aware lint on the tests themselves: `no-unused-vars`, `no-unnecessary-condition` on a `?? ""` over an RTL container's `textContent` (which is typed non-null), and `no-unnecessary-type-assertion` on an `as string` in the render helper. Test files are source here, and they are linted like it.

**The artefact did not move.** 271 modules, 343,658 B of JavaScript, 10,926 B of CSS, **three files**, md5 `cba2825c…` — byte-identical to Task 1.7.7's and unchanged for the fourth story running. The two helper modules and eight test files are unreachable from `index.html`, exactly as the stories files are, and a grep of the emitted JavaScript and CSS for `renderWithContext`, `test-render` and `@testing-library` returns zero. Storybook's own output is unchanged at 299 modules / ~~59 files~~ / 9.3 MB — **63 files when Task 1.12.8 re-took it (2026-09-04)**, the four being `BackendIndicator`'s stories entering the workshop; the disk figure did not move. (`storybook-static` does contain the string `testing-library` — that is Storybook's **own** bundled instrumenter, not ours.)

**Vitest emits nothing that needs ignoring.** No cache directory, no results file, nothing untracked after a run — `coverage/` was already in `.gitignore`, `.prettierignore` and `eslint.config.mjs`'s ignores, and it stays Task 1.9.5's to actually produce. The `test` step of `pnpm verify` went from **0.45 s to 0.80 s** for one real suite of 7 tests (92–131 ms of it Vitest's own reported duration, the rest process startup), and the whole chain is **10.45 s against a 10.00 s baseline** taken immediately before the install on the same tree. Per step: build 3.55 s, lint 3.67 s, `format:check` 2.83 s, `stories` 0.28 s, `env:check` 0.28 s, `test` 0.80 s. **Task 1.9.3's 49 backend tests took `test` to 1.16 s** (218 ms of it Vitest's own duration) on a chain of 10.79 s — build 2.84 s, lint 3.30 s, `format:check` 2.67 s, `stories` 0.26 s, `env:check` 0.26 s. **Task 1.9.4's 47 frontend tests took it to 2.51 s** on a chain of 11.69 s — build 2.24 s, lint 3.69 s, `format:check` 3.07 s, `stories` 0.28 s, `env:check` 0.27 s. The frontend is the expensive third: 1.25 s for the package alone, of which the jsdom environment is ~3.5 s of wall time across the workers and the tests themselves are 663 ms. **Task 1.9.7 re-took the whole chain on the shipping tree and it is 11.78 s** — build 2.44 s, lint 3.66 s, `format:check` 2.79 s, `stories` 0.25 s, `env:check` 0.25 s, `test` **2.36 s** — so the step this story owns went 0.45 s → 2.36 s in total and every other step is inside its own run-to-run variance. Five consecutive stories have now measured the chain total going up and down while the tree only grew (9.3–9.8 s, 10.1 s, 8.77 s, 9.25 s, 11.78 s); read the per-step split.

**`pnpm dev` at the root is three real loops and eight processes.** `packages/shared` sits in `tsc -b --watch --preserveWatchOutput`; `apps/backend` runs `scripts/dev.sh`, which is a second `tsc -b --watch` plus `node --watch dist/index.js`, so the server's own JSON log lines appear prefixed with `apps/backend dev:`; `apps/frontend` runs `vite`. Ctrl-C takes all of it down and leaves no orphaned `node`, `tsc` or `vite` process, and releases both ports — verified in Task 1.2.6 and re-verified across all three packages in Task 1.3.5, counting survivors in the process group rather than trusting the supervisor.

**A clean Ctrl-C at the root is noisy, and the noise is not a failure.** pnpm reports **one** interrupted watcher as `Failed` (not each of them), prints `[ERR_PNPM_RECURSIVE_RUN_FIRST_FAIL] ... Command failed with signal "SIGINT"`, and often adds a spurious `[WARN] Local package.json exists, but node_modules missing, did you mean to install?`. Nothing is missing — `node_modules` was checked immediately afterwards. Do not go looking for a broken install. **Task 1.8.2 tried to fix it and could not, and the package it blames is a race.** `pnpm -r` reports whichever watcher exits first, so the `Failed` line named `packages/shared` in three runs and `apps/frontend` in a fourth; Task 1.8.7 saw `apps/frontend` on a clean Ctrl-C and `packages/shared` on a busy-3000 shutdown — there is nothing truthful for it to say. **Task 1.8.7 also found the warning itself is non-deterministic**: absent on the clean Ctrl-C and present on the busy-3000 shutdown, in the same session. So it is not a line to match on either way. Every lever was measured: `--no-bail` drops the `ERR_PNPM_RECURSIVE_RUN_FIRST_FAIL` block but prints **two** `Failed` lines, keeps the false warning, and stops a genuinely broken dev script aborting the run; `--loglevel error` drops the false warning and suppresses **every** child's streamed output, the server's log included; `--silent` prints nothing at all. The remaining option is a SIGINT-trapping shell wrapper per package, which is two new files in the part of this repository `pnpm verify` reads with nothing. So it stays, Task 1.8.5 documents it, and ADR 0008 records the levers so nobody re-derives them.

`--preserveWatchOutput` is load-bearing rather than cosmetic: without it a `tsc --watch` clears the terminal on every rebuild and takes the other packages' output — including the server's log — with it. **Re-checked in Task 1.8.2 with a positive control, and the check needs a pseudo-terminal**: tsc only clears on a TTY, so a redirected log shows nothing either way. Under a real pty over a start and one rebuild, `tsc -b --watch` emits its clear sequence **twice** and `tsc -b --watch --preserveWatchOutput` emits it **zero** times. Note the sequence is `ESC[2J` followed by `ESC[H`, which is **not** Vite's — see the frontend baseline below. Two tools, two ways of clearing a screen, and a grep written for one reports zero for the other and reads as a pass.

**Editing `apps/backend/package.json` bounces the dev server**, which reads as a bug during unrelated work and is not one. That file is now both a TypeScript program input and a file the running process loads, because the health route imports it for `version` — so adding a dependency triggers a `tsc` rebuild _and_ a `node --watch` restart. Both observed, not reasoned about.

**Root `pnpm dev` builds `packages/shared` twice, and Task 1.8.7 nearly "corrected" that into being wrong.** Once in its own watcher, once through the backend's `tsc -b --watch` following the project reference. Task 1.8.1 read the transcript and concluded the second compile finds nothing to do; **measured, it emits.** One edit to `packages/shared/src/api-error.ts` under root `pnpm dev` rewrites `packages/shared/dist/api-error.js` **twice** and `tsconfig.tsbuildinfo` **twice**, and the control settles it — the same edit with only `pnpm --filter @marketpulse/shared dev` running rewrites each **once**. So a shared edit costs four tsc lines under two package prefixes rather than two, and the doubling is visible rather than hidden. Harmless, and worth knowing because the symptom of it going wrong would be a corrupted `.tsbuildinfo` with no obvious cause. The general lesson is ADR 0007's arriving from the other direction: **a correction to a recorded figure is itself a claim and needs the same measurement the original did** — 1.7.7 rebuilt four commits to catch two wrong corrections after they landed, and this one was caught before.

Every command in this section was executed from a clean clone in Task 1.1.8, the backend's were re-run from a clean build in Task 1.2.6, and all of them again from a second clean clone with a cold pnpm store in Task 1.3.5. `README.md` carries the same set for humans; keep the two in step.

**The dev loop's restart timing has a baseline, so a regression is visible.** Edit to new listener is ~1.1s, of which `SIGTERM` to the new listener is ~100ms (Task 1.2.4 measured 140ms) and the rest is tsc's incremental compile. The drain itself is sub-millisecond. Seconds rather than milliseconds in the signal half means a route acquired a dependency that does not close promptly. Note also that a Ctrl-C in the dev loop now logs `signal received` / `shutdown complete` on the way out — a _silent_ Ctrl-C is the symptom, not the normal case.

**The frontend has its own baseline, and it is a different mechanism.** HMR edit-to-visible-update is **~100–140 ms warm**; the first edit after a server start is ~850 ms and is not the number to regress against (Task 1.3.3). **Re-measured in Task 1.4.6 with the styling pipeline in it, and the two kinds of edit are not the same number:** a **CSS-only** edit lands in **24–130 ms** (median ~72 ms) and a **component** edit in 177–280 ms, first-edit-after-start 977 ms. Both component figures are upper bounds — the measuring tab reported `visibilityState: "hidden"`, which throttles React's scheduler — so compare the two kinds of edit to each other rather than the component number to 1.3.3's. A cheaper way to prove it was HMR and not a reload than the counter below: check `performance.timeOrigin` is unchanged. Two notes on _how_ this is checked, because the obvious method is wrong twice over. First, **editing a heading and watching it change does not test HMR at all** — it passes identically on a full page reload. The check needs component state: add a counter, click it to a non-zero value, edit a _different_ line, and confirm the number survived (done again in Task 1.3.5, then removed). Second, `clearScreen` cannot be verified through a redirected log — Vite only clears on a TTY, so both settings look identical — and the clear sequence is `ESC[1;1H ESC[0J` rather than the `ESC[2J` a grep reaches for first, which reports zero either way and reads as a pass. That is **Vite's** sequence; tsc's is `ESC[2J ESC[H`, measured in Task 1.8.2, so the two tools need two different greps.

**The frontend dev server does not typecheck, and its two failure modes are opposites.** A **type** error is applied as an ordinary HMR update — no overlay, no console error, state preserved — and is caught only by the editor or `pnpm verify`. A **syntax** error fails the oxc transform loudly, logs source context, and leaves the page on its last good render. The first is the dangerous one: a green-looking dev server is not evidence of a compiling tree, which is why `build` runs `tsc -b` before `vite build`.

**Both frontend servers bind IPv6 loopback; the backend binds IPv4.** `vite` and `vite preview` listen on `[::1]`, so `curl http://localhost:5173/` works while `curl http://127.0.0.1:5173/` is connection-refused. `apps/backend` defaults to `127.0.0.1` and is the reverse. Both are "localhost" to a browser and are not to a script — measured in Task 1.3.5, and it matters for anything Story 1.10 or 1.11 writes as a health check.

**Three ports, two of which are decisions.** Backend 3000 and dev server 5173 were chosen; preview 4173 is Vite's default written down explicitly, because `preview` **inherits** `server.strictPort` but **not** `server.port` — measured both ways. `preview.strictPort` is deliberately absent for the opposite reason: it _is_ inherited, and a second copy is one more place for the two to disagree on an upgrade. `strictPort: true` means a busy 5173 or 4173 exits 1 rather than quietly binding the next port up, matching the backend's `EADDRINUSE` behaviour; the deciding reason was Story 1.12's CORS allowlist, which a silently moved port fails as a browser error naming neither the port nor the cause.

**A busy port fails in opposite ways on the two services, and the cheap-looking one is the dangerous one (Task 1.8.4).** Reproduced through root `pnpm dev` with a squatter on each port. **A busy 5173 is seven lines and stops everything** — `strictPort` exits 1, pnpm's fan-out reports `ERR_PNPM_RECURSIVE_RUN_FIRST_FAIL` and takes the other two loops with it, and the command you just ran has exited. **A busy 3000 is sixteen lines and stops nothing**: the record is 16 because the whole of it is an `err` object and `singleLine` deliberately leaves a stack multi-line, so `listen EADDRINUSE: address already in use 127.0.0.1:3000` is **line 4 of 16** — and then `node --watch` catches the exit, prints `Failed running 'dist/index.js'. Waiting for file changes before restarting...`, the frontend carries on serving, and **nothing exits non-zero**. Sixteen lines scroll away behind Vite's banner and what is left is a pair that looks healthy and is half dead. The verbosity is the minor complaint; the survival is the problem, and no amount of rendering work fixes it. **Recovery has two traps**: freeing the port is not enough, because the loop waits for a _file_ change and not for the port (measured — six seconds free, still dead); and `touch`ing a source is not enough either, because tsc's incremental build emits nothing when the content is unchanged and `node --watch` watches `dist/`. A real edit brings the listener back in about a second. One cosmetic artefact, confirmed and not fixed: `server failed to start ` renders with a trailing space, because `singleLine` appends a flattened object that is empty on a record whose only payload is `err`.

**Most root scripts do not fan out, and `build` is now the exception that proves the rule.** `typecheck` is still a single `tsc -b` over the root solution `tsconfig.json`, because the reference graph already orders the work — `pnpm -r run build` would build `packages/shared` three times. **Root `build` is no longer a bare `tsc -b`**: it is `tsc -b && pnpm --filter @marketpulse/frontend exec vite build`, because the frontend's TypeScript half is `noEmit`, so a plain `tsc -b` would typecheck the frontend, emit no bundle, and let `pnpm verify` pass without the bundler ever running. It names one package rather than fanning out, for the reason above — which means **a second frontend package would be silently missed**. Since Task 1.4.5 it names that package **twice**, because `storybook build` is a third clause on the same line, so there are now two places to miss it. Read the root `build` whenever a package is added; it is the one root script that hardcodes a package name. `lint` is a single root `eslint .` because each package's `eslint .` resolves the same root config, so a fan-out starts three ESLint processes each building its own typescript-eslint project service over the same solution. Only `test` and `dev` are genuinely per-package and use `pnpm -r`. Keep the per-package scripts for working on one package; the root ones are the direct call.

The root `tsconfig.json` is a solution file — `files: []` plus three `references`, compiling nothing itself. It does **not** extend `tsconfig.base.json`, and should not: inheriting compiler options would imply it has sources.

**`typecheck` and `build` are the same command on purpose.** Consumers compile against `packages/shared/dist/*.d.ts`, so typechecking this workspace _is_ building it; there is no cheaper correct pass, and a per-package `--noEmit` fan-out is exactly the thing that passes against stale declarations. Both names are kept because they can diverge later without a rename. `packages/shared`'s `typecheck` was `tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json` until Task 1.1.7 and is now `tsc -b` like everything else.

`verify` chains with `&&`, so the first failure is the exit code, and a failing package script propagates up through `pnpm -r` (verified: a package `test` exiting 3 gives root exit 3).

**`tsc -b --clean` deletes the output of the sources that currently exist, so a hand-deleted source orphans its output permanently.** Delete `src/routes/thing.ts` and `dist/routes/thing.{js,d.ts,js.map,d.ts.map}` survive every subsequent `pnpm clean` — the build state is not what drives the deletion. Measured in Task 1.2.6 after the reverse was written down as a guess; Task 1.2.5 found the residue and Task 1.2.6 found the actual mechanism. **Clean before deleting a source file, or delete its output by hand afterwards.** The symptom is stale files in `dist/` that look like a broken build and are not.

**That trap does not reach `apps/frontend`.** Its `dist/` is Vite's, not tsc's, so its `clean` is `tsc -b --clean && rm -rf dist` — and `rm -rf` is content-blind, deleting the directory whatever sources exist. The root's `clean` gained the same second half for the same reason. The worry that motivated re-checking it turned out to be moot anyway: **`build.emptyOutDir` defaults to true** when `outDir` is inside the project root, so Vite empties the directory on every build and hashed assets never accumulate. Measured in Task 1.3.4 after the opposite was assumed. It is a **default rather than a guarantee** — `emptyOutDir: false` brings the whole problem back.

**`apps/backend/dist` is not a runnable artifact on its own, for two independent reasons, and the first hides the second.** Copied elsewhere it fails at import time on `fastify`, before it ever reaches the health route's read of `../../package.json`; give it a reachable `node_modules` and it then fails on the manifest. Both are `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND` before `listen`. The **package directory** — `dist` + `package.json` + `node_modules` — does run outside the workspace, verified. "Copy `dist/` to the server" is the obvious wrong move and the error message names only half the reason. **Task 1.11.2 re-made both failures rather than citing them** and they still hide each other in that order, and it built the real answer: `pnpm deploy --filter @marketpulse/backend --prod --legacy`, inside `apps/backend/Dockerfile`. See the container paragraphs below.

**`apps/frontend/dist` is the opposite, and the asymmetry is the point.** It _is_ a runnable artefact on its own: **four files, and 356,864 B since Task 1.11.5** (it was 355,985 B when Task 1.11.4 made it four) — it was ~~three files~~ from Task 1.4.2 until then (it was two until the first stylesheet; the token layer in Task 1.4.3 grew the stylesheet from 0.07 kB to 3.04 kB, the semantic layer in Task 1.4.4 to 6.22 kB and the components in Task 1.4.5 to 7.21 kB, **none of them adding a fourth, and six stories of clean-clone measurement reproduced three**), no `package.json`, no `node_modules`, zero bare imports left in the bundle. **Task 1.11.5 then moved the bundle for the first time in six stories** — the probe, the URL resolver and `REQUEST_ID_HEADER` took the JavaScript 343,658 → **344,537 B** (`3c886f88…`) on an unchanged 10,926 B stylesheet, so a default build is **356,864 B over four files**. **Task 1.12.2 moved it again** — the client, the `switch` in the probe and `isApiError` took the JavaScript to **346,167 B** (`1b8fc067…`), the stylesheet unchanged at 10,926 B, `index.html` unchanged at 1,101 B but at a new hash (`575f3730…`) because it carries the hashed script filename, so a default build was **358,494 B over four files**. **Task 1.12.3 moved it a third time and _down_** — deleting `health-probe.ts` and its seven console branches costs more than the polling hook adds, so the JavaScript is **346,144 B** (`3d03f7dd…`) on an unchanged 10,926 B stylesheet and an `index.html` of 1,101 B at another new hash (`2efce0ce…`), for **358,471 B over four files**. **Task 1.12.4 then moved the _stylesheet_ for the first time since Task 1.7.6 and left the JavaScript exactly where it was**: three `--service-*` custom properties in `market.css` take the CSS 10,926 → **11,048 B** (`8f6c4f3b…`) while the bundle is **byte-identical at 346,144 B / `3d03f7dd…`**, and `index.html` is 1,101 B at yet another hash (`2f3335bd…`) because it carries the hashed **stylesheet** filename as well as the script's. So the artefact is **358,593 B over four files**. **Task 1.12.5 then moved both, and the stylesheet moved further than the bundle**: wiring the indicator into `AppHeader` makes `BackendIndicator` reachable from `index.html` for the first time, so the JavaScript is **348,124 B** (`d280e167…`) and the CSS **12,128 B** (`134d5dd8…`) — the +1,080 is the whole of `BackendIndicator.module.css` entering the artefact rather than the one `.serviceRegion` rule that task added, which is the reading a glance at the bundle would miss. `index.html` is 1,101 B at another new hash (`177df27d…`), so the artefact is **361,653 B over four files**. **Task 1.13.4 then moved it by ELEVEN bytes** — a `tabIndex={0}` on `Region`, closing the `scrollable-region-focusable` violation the CI browser gate found on its first run — so the JavaScript is **348,135 B** (`b98aeaa5…`) on an unchanged 12,128 B / `134d5dd8…` stylesheet, `index.html` is 1,101 B at another new hash (`07983678…`), and the artefact is **361,664 B over four files at 278 modules**, reproduced byte-for-byte by the runner's fingerprint. That the JavaScript did not move is the check rather than a coincidence: `BackendIndicator` and its stylesheet are reachable only from a stories file, so they are outside the artefact exactly as every other story is, and a grep of both emitted files for `BackendIndicator` and `checking` returns zero. A bundle that shrinks while the application gains behaviour is not a mis-measurement here; console strings are large and a loop is small. **And the deployed build is not that build**: `VITE_API_BASE_URL` is substituted at build time, so the deployed artefact is 344,609 B / `7654c2e0…` / 356,936 B — a 72-byte difference in the JavaScript that Task 1.11.6 closed — and **it is two files rather than one**: `index.html` also differs, at an identical 1,101 B, because it carries the hashed script filename, so a size comparison reports it unchanged. Read the hash. `verify` still builds without the variable and now says so in its own summary; the deploy workflow builds with it and fingerprints that build, and the result downloaded back from the CDN is byte-identical to the hand deploy. **The fourth file is `staticwebapp.config.json` at 300 B**, and it is there because Azure Static Web Apps requires its configuration at the root of the deployed output — so unlike the backend, whose host configuration lives in a platform panel, **the frontend's host configuration is part of its artefact**. ~~The three original files did not move: `index.html` 1,101 B / `eab270a4…`, `assets/index-C-Puqfnm.js` 343,658 B / `cba2825c…`, `assets/index-DFxUCjbx.css` 10,926 B / `f98519e3…`, unchanged since Task 1.7.7.~~ **Two of those three had stopped being true when that sentence was written, and Task 1.11.8 found it by rebuilding rather than by reading** — the paragraph already says three sentences earlier that the JavaScript moved in Task 1.11.5, so it contradicted itself. Only the **stylesheet** is unchanged since Task 1.7.7: 10,926 B / `f98519e3…`. The JavaScript is ~~`index-_IZTTvsJ.js` 344,537 B / `3c886f88…`~~ **`index-BRokic3g.js` 346,167 B / `1b8fc067…` since Task 1.12.2** on a default build, and **`index.html` is 1,101 B / ~~`21577235…`~~ `575f3730…`** — it changed at Task 1.11.5 too, for the mechanism this file already names: **it carries the hashed script filename**, so the filename moving moves the document while a size comparison reports 1,101 B either way. The correction was measured rather than asserted, per this file's own rule: a worktree built at Task 1.11.4's commit (`ce490e9`) reproduces all three recorded values exactly — `index-C-Puqfnm.js` 343,658 B / `cba2825c…` and `index.html` 1,101 B / `eab270a4a1d4…` — so `eab270a4` was right when it was taken and stopped being right one task later. **Do not cite an `index.html` hash without saying which build produced it**: default and deploy builds differ there as well as in the bundle. All three servable files are byte-identical when downloaded back from the CDN, re-verified in Task 1.11.8. It reaches `dist/` through Vite's `publicDir` — the source of truth is `apps/frontend/public/staticwebapp.config.json`, which keeps it inside Prettier's net. Copied outside the workspace and served by `python3 -m http.server`, it renders. Two consequences for Story 1.11 that neither app implies on its own — the emitted asset path is **absolute** (`base` defaults to `/`), so a subpath deployment is a `base` change and a **rebuild** rather than a hosting setting (**one** edit since Task 1.6.5, not two — see below); and rebuilding `packages/shared` does **not** reach a built frontend, because the shared code is inlined at bundle time and the workspace symlink is not part of the artefact. Both measured in Task 1.3.4.

**`pnpm verify` no longer covers every file in the repository, and there are now three gaps of this kind.** `apps/backend/scripts/dev.sh` is checked by nothing: ESLint sees only JS and TS, Prettier has no shell parser and skips it silently, and `tsc` has no view of it. It is a file that starts the development server, so a syntax error in it is a real if minor failure mode — and since Task 1.7.1 it is **worse in kind**: it carries `export LOG_FORMAT="${LOG_FORMAT:-pretty}"`, which is the only configuration value in the application that `pnpm env:check` cannot see, because that check reads `CONFIG_VARIABLES` and the two `.env.example` files and has no view of a shell script. A typo there is not an error, it is a silent fallback to JSON in the dev loop. Re-dated 2026-09-01 in Task 1.7.7 and still accepted. The second is smaller and the same shape: two `clean` scripts now contain `rm -rf` fragments — unchecked shell inside a JSON string, and Task 1.4.5 added `storybook-static` to both. Not worth a `shellcheck` dependency and another `verify` step for one small file and two small strings — a known and dated choice (2026-08-30), not something CI quietly catches. Note `scripts/check-stories.mjs`, `scripts/check-env-example.mjs` and `scripts/check-ready.mjs` are **not** further gaps: all three are JavaScript, so ESLint and Prettier cover them. **Task 1.8.7 added the third of this kind — the fourth counting the unenforced-invariant class below — and it is prose rather than code (dated 2026-09-02).** `README.md` publishes figures — the startup line count, `pnpm ready`'s poll window, both reload timings, the stylesheet's size — and ten distinct intra-document links, and no tool reads either. The two halves are not alike and the decision differs between them: the links are cheaply checkable and were checked (34 headings, 11 links, 10 distinct, **0 broken** — with a slugger that does **not** collapse whitespace, or the correct double-hyphen anchor reads as broken), while **a figure in a sentence has nothing to compare itself against and no tool can check it at all**. This is a decision against evidence rather than a hypothesis: Task 1.8.5 found the stylesheet documented at 9.82 kB against an actual 10,926 B, stale for two stories, and Task 1.8.6 found three more prose figures wrong in one reading. Closing the cheap half alone would make the section look covered while the expensive half stayed open, so an eighth task to build a link checker was rejected as scaffolding ahead of the iteration that needs it. Story 1.10 owns CI and is where it would belong. Story 1.10 carries all three notes.

**All three are unchanged in kind and re-dated 2026-09-03 by Task 1.9.7, and the third one grew — twice, knowingly.** `scripts/dev.sh` is still read by nothing and still carries the one configuration value `pnpm env:check` cannot see; the two `clean` scripts still carry `rm -rf` inside JSON strings. The prose gap is the one Story 1.9 enlarged: **Task 1.9.5 published a three-row table of coverage percentages into both `README.md` and `CLAUDE.md`** that nothing regenerates and nothing checks, and **Task 1.9.6 added a whole section of executed command forms to `README.md`** plus more figures nothing reads — the 49-tests-at-exit-0 `--` result, the `47 skipped`, the 20%-against-30% coverage comparison. Every one of them was **re-executed rather than cited** in Task 1.9.7 and every one reproduced, which is what makes them true today and says nothing about tomorrow: **they go stale the moment a test is added.** Building a checker was rejected again for the reason Story 1.8 rejected it — scaffolding ahead of the iteration that needs it — and Story 1.10 still owns CI. **One gap Story 1.9 closed with a test rather than a `verify` step**, and it is the model for the others: nothing in `verify` could check that a fallible route declared `500: apiErrorSchema`, and `apps/backend/src/server.test.ts` does, by walking the route table through an `onRoute` hook. **And one gap it opened**: `packages/shared` and `apps/backend` now emit a compiled copy of every test file into `dist/` — unreachable to a consumer through the `exports` map (`ERR_PACKAGE_PATH_NOT_EXPORTED`, measured), invisible to the runner through the scoped `include`, and real, with the `tsc -b --clean` orphan rule applying to them. Deleting a test file without cleaning first leaves four files behind permanently.

**Task 1.11.2 enlarged the first gap from one file to three, and one of the three now decides what runs in production (2026-09-03).** `apps/backend/Dockerfile` and the root `.dockerignore` carry the identical signature `scripts/dev.sh` has, measured with the same one-liner rather than assumed: `prettier --file-info` reports **`"inferredParser": null`** for both, and `eslint` reports `File ignored because no matching configuration was supplied`. So the container's base image, its build steps, its `CMD` form and the list of what never enters the build context are all outside `pnpm verify`. **`hadolint` is the tool that would close the Dockerfile half and is declined**, on exactly the one-file argument that declined `shellcheck` and `actionlint` — and with one thing the other two do not have: **BuildKit lints every build itself**, and its two `InvalidDefaultArgInFrom` warnings here are the deliberate no-default `ARG` rather than a finding. The `.dockerignore` half deserves its own sentence because its failure is silent and is a leak: `.env` is gitignored, a build context is assembled from the **working tree** and not from git, so the entry is what stops a developer's `.env` being copied into the builder stage. Nothing checks that the entry is still there. Recorded as declined rather than unconsidered.

**Task 1.11.4 added a platform configuration file and it is the first one that is _inside_ the net, which is the useful contrast (2026-09-03).** `apps/frontend/public/staticwebapp.config.json` decides what the deployed frontend does with every URL nobody built a file for, and `prettier --file-info` reports **`"inferredParser": "json"`** for it — the same one-liner that reports `null` for the `Dockerfile`, the `.dockerignore` and `scripts/dev.sh`. So a malformed edit fails `format:check` by name. What is **not** checked is the half that matters: nothing validates the schema, so a misspelled `navigationFallback`, a `routes` entry with a typo'd key or an `exclude` glob that matches nothing is green locally and silent in production — the same shape as the workflow's unchecked schema, and the same declined-tool answer. **The specific live trap is the `exclude` array**: it is `["/assets/*"]`, which is exact because Vite puts every hashed asset there, and a file added to `apps/frontend/public/` lands at the artefact's **root**, outside it — measured, `/favicon.svg` and `/nunito-sans-regular.woff2` both come back as `index.html` at 200 rather than 404. Anything added to `public/` needs an `exclude` entry in the same change, and only this paragraph and `README.md` say so.

**Task 1.10.7 re-dated all four to 2026-09-03 and re-checked each rather than citing it, which is the only reason the set is worth having.** `apps/backend/scripts/dev.sh` is still read by nothing — `prettier --file-info` gives it `"inferredParser": null` and `eslint` gives it `File ignored because no matching configuration was supplied`, so both tools decline it and only one of them says so — and it still carries `export LOG_FORMAT="${LOG_FORMAT:-pretty}"`, the one configuration value `pnpm env:check` cannot see. The two `clean` scripts still carry `rm -rf` inside a JSON string, in the root (`rm -rf apps/frontend/dist apps/frontend/storybook-static`) and in `apps/frontend` (`rm -rf dist storybook-static`); the other two packages' `clean` is a bare `tsc -b --clean` and is not a third. **Task 1.10.8 re-ran both one-liners on closing and both reproduce.** **`shellcheck` is still not installed**, and the reason is unchanged and now dated twice: one small shell file and two short strings do not justify a new root dependency and a further `verify` step, and Story 1.10 was explicitly told not to add it. The prose gap grew once more and into the worst place it has yet occupied — **Task 1.10.6 put the badge paragraph at the top of `README.md`**, naming the chain's seven steps and its fast tests plus the 10-test process suite, taken from a real run and checked by nothing thereafter. It goes stale the moment a test is added, and it is now the most-read prose in the repository, sitting under a green badge. See the link-check paragraphs below for what was decided about it.

**There is a fifth gap and it is only half a gap, re-checked rather than cited by Task 1.10.7 (2026-09-03).** `.github/workflows/verify.yml` arrived in Task 1.10.1 as `ci.yml`, and the reflex is to record it beside `scripts/dev.sh` as another file no tool reads. That is wrong in one direction, and the measurement is a one-liner rather than a probe: `prettier --file-info` reports `"inferredParser": "yaml"` for the workflow and **`"inferredParser": null` for `scripts/dev.sh`** — the same command separating the two gaps, which is why they are two entries rather than one. Re-proved end to end anyway: a badly-formatted probe workflow dropped into `.github/workflows/` fails `pnpm format:check` by name at exit 1, and the failure goes away when it does. So the file's _formatting_ is inside the net and its _schema_ is not — ESLint reports `File ignored because no matching configuration was supplied`, and a misspelled key, an action reference that does not resolve, or a `runs-on` label GitHub retires are all green locally and red only on the runner. `actionlint` is the tool that would close it and is **declined**, for the reason `shellcheck` is: one file, against a new root dependency and a further `verify` step. Dated 2026-09-03, and recorded as declined rather than unconsidered.

**Inside that half-gap was the part no tool in or out of this repository watched, and it is not the schema — Task 1.11.6 closed it.** The workflows carry **five** distinct third-party actions pinned to commit SHAs across **eight** uses — counted out of `.github/workflows/` rather than copied from a task write-up, which is the instruction, because this number has been wrong once and grows whenever a step is added: `actions/checkout@3d3c42e5…` (v7.0.1), `actions/setup-node@82076278…` (v7.0.0), `actions/cache@55cc8345…` (v6.1.0), `actions/upload-artifact@043fb46d…` (v7.0.1) and, since `deploy.yml`, `azure/login@7ddb5af1…` (v3.0.2). Every one is bumped by hand, because a SHA does not follow security releases — which is the whole reason they are pinned. `actionlint` would not check them either, and **a stale pin is invisible in every way a stale dependency is not**: `pnpm outdated` has no view of a YAML file, and the lockfile has no view of GitHub. Task 1.10.7 declined Dependabot on a one-file argument with **a fifth action** as the stated reversal trigger; `azure/login` is the fifth, the trigger fired, and the decision was taken rather than left to drift past itself. **`.github/dependabot.yml` now exists**, `github-actions` only, weekly, opening pull requests and merging none — and note it is a **file** rather than the repository setting that note assumed, so unlike the required status check it _is_ visible in a diff. npm is deliberately not enabled: the pnpm workspace has a lockfile, an `allowBuilds` policy and a TypeScript pin held back on purpose, and a bot against that is a decision nobody has taken. The reversal trigger for that half is a dependency here gaining a published advisory.

**The link check was decided rather than deferred a third time, and the answer is no — on evidence that only exists now (2026-09-03).** Task 1.10.7 built the checker before deciding, over every tracked Markdown file rather than `README.md` alone, and read the result: **110 documents, 210 cross-file links and 13 intra-document anchor links (12 distinct), and 0 broken.** **Task 1.10.8 re-ran it on its own output and it is 110 documents, 214 cross-file links, 22 anchor links, 0 broken** — the sixth clean reading, and the two counts moved one task apart, which is a figure rotting inside the very paragraph that says figures rot. So the empirical record across ten stories is that **the half that is cheap to check has never once been wrong, and the half that cannot be checked at all is wrong nearly every time it is read** — Task 1.8.5 found the stylesheet documented at 9.82 kB against an actual 10,926 B and stale for two stories, Task 1.8.6 found three more wrong figures in a single reading, and this task found a fourth (below). A checker in `verify` would be a gating step guarding the one thing that has never rotted, and its presence is what would make the section _look_ covered while the expensive half stayed open — which is exactly the argument Stories 1.8 and 1.9 used, now with numbers under it rather than intuition. **The reversal trigger is a broken link actually shipping** — found by a reader rather than by a task — **or documentation gaining generated content whose links are not hand-written.** If it is ever built it is an **eighth `pnpm verify` step** and a plain-JavaScript file under `scripts/` beside `check-stories.mjs` and `check-env-example.mjs`, never a workflow step, because a CI-only check forks the definition of "verified"; and it has a ready-made failure case in the double-hyphen anchors, so the "made to fail first" rule costs nothing.

**And the prose half rotted again on this very reading, which is the whole point of the paragraph above.** Task 1.10.6 recorded `README.md` at **42 headings**; it has **36**. The difference is six `#` comment lines inside fenced code blocks — a naive `grep -cE '^#{1,6} '` counts them and a correct slugger does not, so the recorded figure was a count of the wrong thing rather than a count that went stale. The link figures from the same reading reproduce exactly (13 links, 12 distinct, 0 broken), and the double-hyphen trap reproduced a **fourth** time: `#pnpm-ready--knowing-the-pair-is-up` and `#pnpm-coverage--on-demand-and-never-in-verify` are both linked and both correct, and a slugger that collapses whitespace reports both as broken. Do not cite 42, and do not cite 36 either — count it with fences stripped.

**There is a sixth thing nothing in this repository checks and it is not a file at all (2026-09-03).** `verify` is a **required status check on `main`**, through repository ruleset `main`, **id 22160620**, `enforcement: active`, scoped to `~DEFAULT_BRANCH` — re-read from the API rather than cited. It requires a pull request and the `verify` check — **and since Task 1.13.4 the `e2e` check as well, so a red browser journey blocks a merge exactly as a red chain does; a reader finding only `verify` required should read that as the browser gate having been REMOVED rather than never set.** Nothing in the tree records it, no tool reads it, and `pnpm verify` cannot see it, so **the repository has no way to detect its own gate being switched off**: a future reader finding the gate absent cannot tell whether it was removed or never set, and this paragraph plus ADR 0010 are the only durable copy. Four things about it fail silently. It keys on the **job** name, so renaming the job in `verify.yml` un-requires it with no error anywhere — which is why Task 1.10.2 settled the workflow, job and file names four tasks before the badge needed them. **Admin bypass is retained** (`bypass_actors`: RepositoryRole 5, `bypass_mode: always`), so the gate is a decision to override rather than a wall, and a merged red run leaves no trace in any file. And two GitHub defaults were changed at creation: `require_extra_approval_for_unattributed_changes` is **off** — it defaults **on**, and with `required_approving_review_count: 0` it blocks the maintainer's own pull request over a co-author trailer that resolves to no account — and `strict_required_status_checks_policy` is **off**, because a `pull_request` run already verifies the merge commit rather than the branch tip. What the gate binds is the chain and **nothing about coverage**, because the job is green whatever the `continue-on-error` step did.

**Task 1.11.8 re-ran the whole list by measurement and it is still six _kinds_ — this story enlarged four of them and closed none (2026-09-03).** Every reading below is a one-liner re-run rather than a citation, because that is the only thing that has ever caught this list drifting. `prettier --file-info` reports **`"inferredParser": null`** for `apps/backend/scripts/dev.sh`, `apps/backend/Dockerfile` and the root `.dockerignore`, and ESLint reports `File ignored because no matching configuration was supplied` for all three — so **the first kind is three files**, not one, and the container's base image, its build steps, its `CMD` form and the list of what never enters the build context are all outside `pnpm verify`. **`hadolint` is declined** on the one-file argument that declined `shellcheck`, with one thing the other two do not have: **BuildKit lints every build itself**, and its two `InvalidDefaultArgInFrom` warnings here are the deliberate no-default `ARG` rather than a finding. That is the third tool decline and it is dated like the other two.

**The fifth kind is now FIVE files since Task 2.1.2 added `compose.yaml` (2026-09-04), and the answer to "one entry or two" is still one.** `.github/workflows/verify.yml`, `.github/workflows/deploy.yml`, `.github/dependabot.yml`, `apps/frontend/public/staticwebapp.config.json` and **`compose.yaml`** all report a parser to Prettier — `yaml`, `yaml`, `yaml`, **`json`**, `yaml` — measured with the same one-liner rather than assumed, and ESLint reports `File ignored because no matching configuration was supplied` for the new one exactly as it does for the rest. So a malformed edit to the local database's definition fails `format:check` by name, and a misspelled Compose key, a healthcheck testing the wrong thing, or a volume mounted at a path the image does not use are all green locally — so a malformed edit to any of them fails `format:check` by name, and **nothing validates any of their schemas**. They are one entry because the failure is identical in all four: formatting checked, meaning not. Splitting the deploy workflow out would suggest its schema is watched by something different, and it is not. What `deploy.yml` additionally carries is not a schema problem at all and belongs in the sixth kind below. The **live trap in `staticwebapp.config.json` is the `exclude` array**: it is `["/assets/*"]`, exact only because Vite puts every hashed asset there, and a file added to `apps/frontend/public/` lands at the artefact's **root** outside it, where a miss is answered with `index.html` at 200. **A check for that is declined and the decision is stated rather than implied**: with exactly one file in `public/` today — the config file itself, which the platform consumes — it is scaffolding ahead of the iteration that needs it. The reversal trigger is a **second** file in `public/`, which is the moment the check stops being vacuous.

**The third kind gained one entry, and it was counted rather than described.** The frontend's environment boundary has three enforcers now (`envPrefix` at build time, `pnpm env:check` on the example file, and the compiler at the reading site via `strictImportMetaEnv`), which is one more than it had — and the cost is that **every variable is declared in two places and nothing checks the pair**: `scripts/check-env-example.mjs` reads `.env.example` and has no view of `apps/frontend/src/vite-env.d.ts`. **That pair is one variable long today and the two agree**, verified. Extending the script to parse the `.d.ts` is a small change to a file `verify` already runs, and it is **deferred to Story 1.12**, which brings the second variable and is where a pair becomes a set. Deferring is cheap precisely because the set is one.

**The sixth kind is now the largest instance in the project, and it is not the ruleset.** `deploy.yml` uses `update` and never `create`, and deliberately does not restate the app's configuration, so **the three HTTP probes, `minReplicas: 1`, the ingress target port, `HOST=0.0.0.0` and `CORS_ORIGIN` exist only in the platform** — re-read off the live app in Task 1.11.8 rather than cited, because Task 1.11.7 had to read them to work at all. The sharpest instance is that **the probes pin port 3000 while `PORT` is an environment variable**, so the two can be made to disagree from the platform panel with nothing here noticing. **A script that reads the deployed configuration back and diffs it against a recorded expectation is declined**, and the reason is structural rather than economic: it **cannot be a `verify` step**, because `verify` runs with no credentials, so building one forks the definition of "verified" in the exact way Stories 1.1 and 1.10 exist to prevent. Prose is the answer here as it is for the ruleset — ADR 0011 and `HOSTING.md` are the durable copy. The reversal trigger is a second environment, or one of these values being found changed with nobody able to say why. **Task 2.1.6 added six more environment variables to it on 2026-09-05, taking the app to eleven, and one of them is now the most dangerous entry in the set**: `DATABASE_AUTH=entra` and `DATABASE_SSL=verify-full` are a **pair Task 2.1.3's cross-variable check enforces at startup**, so a revision that sets the first and forgets the second does not connect insecurely — it fails to start, on a liveness-probed platform, sitting at `Activating` for ten minutes. That is the right failure and it is a loud one naming the variable, and it means **every change to this app's database configuration is one `az containerapp update` or none**. The table of all six is in `HOSTING.md` and is their only durable copy. **Task 2.1.5 roughly doubled this category on 2026-09-05 and none of it is in any file**: the database's **two firewall rules**, its **Entra administrator**, the **`marketpulse-backend` role** created by a manual `pgaadauth_create_principal` call, the **action group and two metric alerts**, and the **`CanNotDelete` lock**. **The sharpest instance is the role**, because it is not a setting that can be re-read and compared — it is a **one-off SQL statement that must be re-run by hand if the server is ever re-created**, and `HOSTING.md` is its only copy. That is also the argument the lock rests on: what deletion destroys is not the data, which is re-derivable from Alpaca, but the bootstrap that exists nowhere in this tree.

**And the pins were re-counted out of the files rather than copied: five distinct actions across ~~eight~~ ~~THIRTEEN~~ **EIGHTEEN** uses since Task 1.13.5's `check-deployed` job (six distinct `uses:` references, because `actions/cache/restore` is a sub-action of `actions/cache` at the same SHA)** — `actions/checkout`, `actions/setup-node` and `actions/cache` twice each, `actions/upload-artifact` and `azure/login` once each, every one on a 40-character commit SHA. `.github/dependabot.yml` watches them; npm is still deliberately not enabled.

**Both of the pipeline's derived readouts are diagnostics rather than checks, and saying so is the point — they are stated invariants of the third kind, inside the thing this story built.** The `Verify` step's per-step split is **derived** from pnpm's own output: the names are parsed out of the first `$ pnpm run build && pnpm run lint && …` line and the boundaries are the timestamps of the root-level `$ ` lines. Nothing checks that pnpm keeps printing either, so a pnpm upgrade that changes the announcement format prints **nothing, or the wrong names, on a run that is still green**. That is harmless by construction and the sentence to remember is: **the split is diagnostics, not a check, and a silently empty split is not a failing build** — the exit code is the chain's and never the parser's. Since Task 1.10.6 it has a second consumer and a slightly worse failure mode, because the same derived file renders the job summary's first section, so a format change empties a section a reviewer reads rather than a block in a log nobody opens. **Re-read from a real run rather than cited** (`main`, run 33709042823): seven rows — build 8,281 / lint 7,715 / `format:check` 6,307 / `stories` 446 / `env:check` 448 / `test` 6,429 / **`test:process` 8,910**, TOTAL 39,441 ms — with the workflow naming no step, which is the third sighting of the property and the second on a chain the file has never heard of. The coverage step's per-package table is derived the same way, from `pnpm -r`'s `<package-dir> coverage:` line prefixes, and **its failure is quieter**: it sits inside a `continue-on-error` step, so a derivation matching nothing produces an annotation on a **green** run. Everything the coverage step reports is diagnostics that can only ever raise an annotation — with one exception, which is a real assertion: the two 0% entrypoints are checked for **presence in the report** (`in the denominator: apps/backend/coverage/src/index.ts.html`, and the frontend's `main.tsx.html`, both re-read from that run). Presence and not a percentage, which is the answer to the third gap's own complaint that a stated invariant quietly stops being enforced. One more of the same class, and it is one line: the `Install` step prints `cache-hit` and then a sentence telling the reader to use pnpm's `reused`/`downloaded` counts to tell a restore-key hit from a total miss. That is true of `actions/cache` v6.1.0, read out of its own `action.yml`, and nothing checks it survives a SHA bump — harmless in the same way, because the cache is diagnostics around an install that succeeds or fails on its own.

**`continue-on-error` reports a step's result in a way that reads like a swallowed failure and is not, and it is written down here so nobody files it as one.** A step marked `continue-on-error` reports `conclusion: success` however it exited — in the API and in the UI — and the real result lives in `steps.<id>.outcome`. That is the intended shape rather than a defect: no coverage outcome may turn the tick red, which is what makes "the green tick certifies the chain and not coverage" structural rather than documented. The evidence is Task 1.10.4's: a throwaway commit made the entrypoint assertion fail, the run's conclusion stayed `success`, and two `failure` annotations were the visible trace. Two rules follow. **Never write a later step's `if:` against a `continue-on-error` step's `conclusion`**, and never read the absence of a marked step as "coverage was fine".

**The pipeline is GitHub Actions, and that was taken as a decision rather than inherited.** The alternatives were a provider-agnostic runner script (a second place the pipeline lives, for a portability nobody needs) and GitLab/Circle (a second host beside the one the work already flows through). `origin` is `github.com/theSmaw/marketpulse` and every story since 1.1 was delivered as a pull request against it. **The reversal cost is one YAML file**, and it stays that way only because the pipeline runs `pnpm verify` by name and defines nothing of its own (Task 1.10.2) — the moment a workflow re-lists the tools, the reversal cost becomes the definition of "verified".

**All three actions are pinned to a commit SHA and the runner label is not, deliberately.** `actions/checkout@3d3c42e5…` (v7.0.1), `actions/setup-node@82076278…` (v7.0.0) and — since Task 1.10.3 — `actions/cache@55cc8345…` (v6.1.0) are third-party code running with the repository checked out, so they take the same position `allowBuilds` takes one layer down; the cost is that a SHA does not follow security releases and has to be bumped by hand. `ubuntu-latest` stays floating for the opposite reason: it is GitHub's own image, and a pinned image ages out of support and fails hard. The job resolved to `ubuntu-24.04`, image `20260831.293.1`, `Linux 6.17.0-1022-azure x86_64`.

**Four Story 1.1 decisions had never run anywhere but a Mac, and all four hold.** Node came from `.nvmrc` as `v24.20.0` with no version literal in the workflow; pnpm came from `packageManager` as `11.24.0` through `corepack enable` with no pnpm install step and no pnpm action — Corepack fetches the tarball on the runner, which is ~2 s inside the toolchain step and is the reason that step is not instant. `--frozen-lockfile` is explicit rather than left to CI's default. And **`allowBuilds`' one entry did what it exists for**: `esbuild@0.28.2 postinstall$ node install.js` ran, `@esbuild+linux-x64@0.28.2` is in the tree, and the binary answers `0.28.2` when executed — which is the test, since a skipped install script leaves a shim that cannot. No new `allowBuilds` entry was needed.
